\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\xi):=\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am-2k}{4}}}\varphi\Big(\frac{\xi}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}-u-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1)\Big)e^{-in\xi 2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}

with
\varphi
a suitable smooth function compactly supported and then decompose

(124)

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{\ell+1}(\xi):=\widehat{f}(\xi)\phi({\xi\over 2^{am-k}}-(\ell+1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\widehat{f}_{\ell+1},\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\xi)=\sum_{2\cdot 2^{\frac{am}{2}}<u\leq 3\cdot 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\widehat{f}_{\ell+1},\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\xi),

and

(125)

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{\ell-1}(\eta):=\widehat{g}(\eta)\phi({\eta\over 2^{am-k}}-(\ell-1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v,p\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\widehat{g}_{\ell-1},\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\eta)=\sum_{1\leq v\leq 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\sum_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\widehat{g}_{\ell-1},\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\eta),

where in the above we made use of the support properties for
\widehat{f}_{\ell+1}
,
\widehat{g}_{\ell-1}
and
\varphi
.

If we now insert (
124
) and (
125
) back to (
121
) we deduce that

(126)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\widehat{f}_{\ell+1},\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\langle\widehat{g}_{\ell-1},\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}(x),

with

(127)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\iint e^{ic_{a}(\xi-\eta)^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}}e^{i\xi x}e^{i\eta x}

\displaystyle\cdot\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\xi)\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}(\eta)\psi\Big(\frac{\xi-\eta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)d\xi d\eta.

This ends the linearized wave-packet decomposition of our operator, of course, up to a better understanding of the kernel
\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}
.

4.2.5.
Analyzing the kernel

In this section we focus on obtaining an explicit form for the kernel
\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}
by exploiting the time-frequency localization of the wave-packets in relation to the oscillatory factor
\displaystyle e^{ic_{a}(\xi-\eta)^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}}
in (
127
).

Notice that the change of variables
\xi\rightarrow 2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\xi
and
\eta\rightarrow 2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\eta
in (
127
) together with the definition of the wave-packets (
123
) allows us to rewrite the above as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}(x)=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{k}}\iint e^{ic_{a}\frac{\big(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(\xi-\eta)\big)^{a^{\prime}}}{\lambda(x)^{1/(a-1)}}}\cdot e^{i(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\xi x+2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\eta x-\xi n-\eta p)}

\displaystyle\cdot\varphi(\xi-u-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))\varphi(\eta-v-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))\psi\Big(\frac{\xi-\eta}{2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\Big)d\xi d\eta.

Applying now a second change of variables
\xi-\eta=\xi_{1}
and
\xi+\eta=\eta_{1}
we rewrite the kernel
\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}(x)
as

(128)

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{k}}\iint e^{ic_{a}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\xi_{1})^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}}e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\eta_{1}x}e^{-i\frac{\xi_{1}+\eta_{1}}{2}n}e^{-i\frac{\eta_{1}-\xi_{1}}{2}p}

\displaystyle\cdot\varphi\Big(\frac{\xi_{1}+\eta_{1}}{2}-u-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1)\Big)\varphi\Big(\frac{\eta_{1}-\xi_{1}}{2}-v-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1)\Big)\psi\Big(\frac{\xi_{1}}{2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\Big)d\xi_{1}d\eta_{1}.

We claim that the expression under the integral sign behaves essentially as a tensor product of the form
a(\xi_{1})b(\eta_{1})
for some suitable functions
a
and
b
. Indeed, given the support localization for the function
\varphi
our kernel can be safely approximated as

(129)

\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}(x)\approx\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}w_{k,\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)(x)\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}}(x),

where here
78
78

78

For simplicity of the exposition, we abuse the notation and in the definitions of
w_{k,\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)(x)
and
\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am-2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}}(x)
we are using the same symbol
\varphi
as the one introduced earlier though of course, strictly speaking, the functions involved in these definitions while having similar properties do not need to agree with
\varphi
.

(130)

\displaystyle w_{k,\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)(x):=\int\varphi(\xi_{1}-(u-v))e^{i\frac{p-n}{2}\xi_{1}}e^{ic_{a}2^{({am\over 2}-k)a^{\prime}}\xi_{1}^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-{1\over{a-1}}}}\psi\Big(\frac{\xi_{1}}{2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\Big)d\xi_{1},

and the wave-packet
\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am-2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}}(x)
stands for the expression

(131)

\int e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\eta_{1}x}e^{-i\frac{p+n}{2}\eta_{1}}\varphi(\eta_{1}-(u+v)-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(2\ell-2))d\eta_{1}.

The rigorous, formal approach to the informal statement (
129
) follows some standard reasonings in Fourier analysis and thus, in what follows, we will only provide an outline of them:

Since
\varphi
is smooth and compactly supported – for the sake of the argument one may assume without loss of generality that

\textrm{supp}\varphi\subseteq[0,1]
– we can write pointwise within
[0,1]
the equality between
\varphi
and its Fourier series, that is

\varphi(\zeta)=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{s}e^{is\zeta},\>\>\>\>\forall\>\zeta\in[0,1],

where the coefficients
(c_{s})_{s}
are fast-decaying (in a way that agrees with the regularity of
\varphi
).

Deduce from the above that on the support of the function
79
79

79

Here we abuse the notation as we allow the function
\varphi
to slightly change from line to line.

(\xi_{1},\eta_{1})\mapsto\varphi(\xi_{1}-(u-v))\varphi(\eta_{1}-(u+v)-2^{am\over 2}(2\ell-2))
one has that

\varphi(\frac{\xi_{1}+\eta_{1}}{2}-u-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))\varphi(\frac{\eta_{1}-\xi_{1}}{2}-v-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))

equals

\displaystyle\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{s}c_{t}e^{is(\frac{\xi_{1}+\eta_{1}}{2}-u-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))}e^{it(\frac{\eta_{1}-\xi_{1}}{2}-v-2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))}.

Thus, (
129
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{u,v,n,p}_{m,k,\ell}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{s}c_{t}\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}w_{k,\frac{s-t}{2}+\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)(x)\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}-\frac{s+t}{2}}(x)

\displaystyle e^{-is(u+2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))}e^{-it(v+2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1))}

\displaystyle=:\sum_{s,t\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{s}c_{t}\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{u,v,n+t,p+s}_{m,k,\ell}(x).

Due to the fast decay of the coefficients
\{c_{s}\}_{s}
we can focus on the case
s=t=0
, justifying our claim (
129
).

We conclude this section with the following remark: by a simple inspection of (
129
) we notice that the behavior of the rough linearizing function is now separated and isolated within the term
w_{k,\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)
which can be regarded as a weight attached to the standard wave-packet
\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}}
.

4.2.6.
Achieving the final form of our first model

Based on the previous section’s reasonings, we were able to reduce the initial operator
T_{m,k}(f,g)
defined in (
120
) to the study of an expression realized as a superposition of simpler operators that reads as
80
80

80

In this precise context, for notational simplicity, we are abusing our convention on the notation
v\sim 2^{am\over 2}
(which as defined originally is equivalent with
|v|\sim 2^{am\over 2}
). Indeed, in formula (
132
) the domain of the summation
\{u,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\}
is in fact restricted to
2^{\frac{am}{2}}<u\leq 2\cdot 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
and
-\frac{3}{2}2^{am\over 2}<v\leq-\frac{1}{2}2^{\frac{am}{2}}
.

(132)

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sum_{n,p\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\langle\hat{f}_{\ell+1},\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\langle\hat{g}_{\ell-1},\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}}(x)w_{k,\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sum_{n,p\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sum_{u,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}

\displaystyle\langle\hat{f}_{\ell+1},\varphi^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle\langle\hat{g}_{\ell-1},\varphi^{v,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{u+v,\frac{p+n}{2}}(x)w_{k,\frac{p-n}{2},\frac{v-u}{2}}^{e}(\lambda)(x).

By applying Parseval’s identity and the change of variables
(u,v,n,p)\to(u-v,u+v,p-n,p+n)
we have that the expression in (
132
) may be rewritten as

(133)

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p}(x)w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x).

The first task for this section is to show that the study of (
133
) can be further reduced to a simpler expression – which, for notational simplicity, will be redefined as
T_{m,k}
– and which involves the summation only over
n\sim_{a}2^{\frac{am}{2}}
.
81
81

81

We remind here that the definition of
\sim_{a}
was provided in footnote
53
.
82
82

82

This will be an immediate consequence of the structure of the decaying term in the right-hand side of (
140
).
Indeed, our claim is that it is enough to consider
83
83

83

From now on, for notational simplicity, we will drop the
a
dependence in
\sim_{a}
. However, it should be noted that, strictly speaking, whenever we read
n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
we have in fact that
n,p\sim_{a}2^{\frac{am}{2}}
.

(134)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\\
p\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p}(x)w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle.

If is to believe for a moment (
134
), then the restriction
n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
naturally
84
84

84

Notice the structure
p-n
and
p+n
in the Gabor coefficients
\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle
and
\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle
, respectively. The deeper, geometric motivation for the regrouping of the sum in
p
will become transparent in Section
4.5
.
invites to a regrouping of the sum in
p
in subcomponents spanning precisely the reference length
2^{\frac{am}{2}}
. More precisely, we will write the set of integers as a disjoint union of intervals containing
2^{\frac{am}{2}}
consecutive integers, and using the notation

(135)

p_{r}:=2^{\frac{am}{2}}(r-1)+p,\quad\text{ with }\quad r\in\mathbb{Z}\quad\textrm{and}\quad 2^{am\over 2}\leq p<2\cdot 2^{am\over 2}

we will rewrite (
134
) as

(136)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle.

Returning now to (
134
), the key for proving our claim resides in the behavior of the oscillatory term

(137)

\displaystyle\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle=\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\int\varphi(\xi+2v)e^{in\xi}e^{ic_{a}2^{({am\over 2}-k)a^{\prime}}\xi^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-{1\over{a-1}}}}\psi\Big(\frac{\xi}{2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\Big)d\xi.

Denote now the phase in (
137
) by

(138)

\nu(\xi):=n\xi+c_{a}2^{({am\over 2}-k)a^{\prime}}\xi^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-{1\over{a-1}}},

and integrate by parts twice in the right-hand side of (
137
) in order to obtain under the integral sign a decaying factor of the form
\frac{1}{1+|\nu^{\prime}(\xi)|^{2}}
. Once here, denoting by
\xi_{c}
the
85
85

85

Up to a sign, this real number is uniquely determined.
critical point of
\nu
(
i.e.
,
\nu^{\prime}(\xi_{c})=0
) we have two situations:

•

either
|\xi_{c}|\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
case in which, using the phase linearization performed in Section
4.2.3
we have by construction that
|\nu^{\prime\prime}(\xi)|\sim 1
for
|\xi|\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
and hence, via a Taylor series argument, we deduce that
|\nu^{\prime}(\xi)|\sim|\xi-\xi_{c}|\sim|\xi_{c}+2v|
;

•

or else we must have
|\nu^{\prime}(\xi)|\gtrsim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
.

In either of the above situations, observing that

(139)

\xi_{c}=\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}},

we obtain a first time-frequency correlation condition as given by

(140)

\displaystyle\big|\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\frac{1}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{2}}

\displaystyle=:w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)

Now the decay in (
140
) offers us the intuition that the main contribution in (
133
) is expected to come from parameters
n
and
v
satisfying

-2v\approx\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}.

However, since
v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
, that effectively restricts
n
to
|n|\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
which by symmetry may be reduced to
n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
thus providing an informal argument supporting our claim in (
134
).

The formal, rigorous argument is presented now. First, recall from Section
3.4
that we only need to prove operator bounds for
\sum_{k}T_{m,k}
in the local-
L^{2}
range (
i.e.

2<p,q\leq\infty
) with a
2^{-\delta am}
decay. Thus, our claim is a consequence of the following dualized statement:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}=\mathfrak{N}_{m}
be the subset of indices
86
86

86

Here we use
A\ll_{a}B
to denote the inequality
A\leq c_{a}B
for a constant
c_{a}>0
depending on
a
and sufficiently small that any subsequent arguments appealing to this inequality are enabled (with the obvious modifications for the situation
A\gg_{a}B
).

\mathfrak{N}:=\{n\in\mathbb{Z}:|n|\ll_{a}2^{\frac{am}{2}}\textrm{ or }|n|\gg_{a}2^{\frac{am}{2}}\}.

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
define now
the trilinear form
87
87

87

This is obtained from (
133
) by restricting
n
to
\mathfrak{N}
and then dualizing against the function
h
.

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}^{\mathfrak{N}}(f,g,h):=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathfrak{N}\\
p\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\int\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)dx.

Then for every
N>0
and any exponents
p,q,r
such that
2<p,q\leq\infty
and
1/p+1/q=1/r
we have

\big\|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}^{\mathfrak{N}}\big\|_{L^{p}\times L^{q}\times L^{r^{\prime}}\to{\mathbb{C}}}\lesssim_{a,p,q,N}2^{-Nam}

uniformly in
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Recalling the definition of
\nu(\xi)
in (
138
) we have

\nu^{\prime}(\xi)=n+c_{a}a^{\prime}2^{({am\over 2}-k)a^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{\xi}{\lambda(x)}\Big)^{{1\over{a-1}}}

with
|\xi|\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
(because of the support of
\psi
); for
n\in\mathfrak{N}
the phase function has no critical point, since

|\nu^{\prime}(\xi)|\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
if
|n|\ll_{a}2^{\frac{am}{2}}
and

|\nu^{\prime}(\xi)|\sim|n|
if
|n|\gg_{a}2^{\frac{am}{2}}
. We can thus summarize this as

|\nu^{\prime}(\xi)|\sim(2^{\frac{am}{2}}+|n|).

Therefore, repeated integration by parts in (
137
) yields for any
88
88

88

The value of
N
is allowed to vary from line to line.

N>0

\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))|w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)|\lesssim_{N}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}+|n|)^{-N}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x)).

For any interval
I
, we introduce now the
L^{\infty}
-normalised weight adapted to
I
as

(141)

\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x):=\Big(1+\frac{\mathrm{dist}(x,I)}{|I|}\Big)^{-M}

for some large
M>0
– also allowed to change from line to line. Recalling the definition of our wave-packet in (
123
) we have

|\check{\varphi}^{2u,p}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}(x)|\lesssim 2^{\frac{am-2k}{4}}\tilde{\chi}_{I^{p}_{k}}(x),

where
I^{p}_{k}
denotes the interval

(142)

I^{p}_{k}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}:2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p\in[0,1]\}=\Big[\frac{p}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}},\frac{p+1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\Big].

Taking absolute values and applying the pointwise bounds above we have then for the trilinear form

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}^{\mathfrak{N}}(f,g,h)|\lesssim 2^{-k}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathfrak{N}\\
p\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}|\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle||\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle|

\displaystyle\cdot(2^{\frac{am}{2}}+|n|)^{-N}\int\tilde{\chi}_{I^{p}_{k}}(x)|h(x)|\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))dx,

where the last factor does not depend anymore on
u,v,\ell
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz in
u,v
and
\ell
yields without difficulty

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}^{\mathfrak{N}}(f,g,h)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathfrak{N}\\
p\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Big(\int|f(y)|^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p-n}}(y)dy\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int|g(z)|^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p+n}}(z)dz\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\cdot(2^{\frac{am}{2}}+|n|)^{-N}\int\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p}}(x)|h(x)|\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))dx.

Now for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
, standard reasonings imply that

\displaystyle\Big(\int|f(y)|^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p-n}}(y)dy\Big)^{1/2}\leq 2^{\frac{1}{2}(k-{am\over 2})}\Big(|n|+\frac{\text{dist}(x,I_{k}^{p})}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\Big)^{1\over 2}M_{2}(f)(x),

with a similar inequality for the average on
g
(here
M_{2}(f)=M(|f|^{2})^{1/2}
).
Deduce from here that

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}^{\mathfrak{N}}(f,g,h)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n\in\mathfrak{N}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}+|n|)^{-N}\int\Big(|n|+\frac{\text{dist}(x,I_{k}^{p})}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\Big)M_{2}(f)(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot M_{2}(g)(x)\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p}}(x)|h(x)|\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))dx

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-Nam}\sum_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}\int M_{2}(f)(x)M_{2}(g)(x)\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p}}(x)|h(x)|\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))dx.

Given the definition of
\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p}}
the sum in
p
is
\lesssim 1
. The factor of
\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))
enables us to further sum over
k
and thus

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}^{\mathfrak{N}}(f,g,h)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-Nam}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\int M_{2}(f)(x)M_{2}(g)(x)|h(x)|\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))dx

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-Nam}\int M_{2}(f)(x)M_{2}(g)(x)|h(x)|dx.

Via Hölder, the latter expression is now trivially bounded in the stated range
2<p,q\leq\infty
and
1/p+1/q=1/r
thus ending our proof.
∎

We now record the dualized version of what we achieved via our reductions:

(143)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}(f,g,h):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}(f,g,h):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle T_{m,k}(f,g),h\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{m\in\mathbb{N}}\atop{k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{n,p,u,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle\langle h,\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda)}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)\rangle.

As already mentioned in Section
4.1
, the major problem that we are confronted with when dealing with the above model is the
failure of the
m
-decay absolute summability property – see the Appendix A for a proof. As a consequence, we have to design an alternative route that will help us in Section
5
to extract the cancellation encoded in the curvature of the phase in our operator’s kernel. This route is the subject of our next section.

4.3.
The high resolution model (II): a continuous phase-linearized spatial model

4.3.1.
Motivation and intuition

The aim of this subsection is to highlight a simple though deep manifestation of the fact the trigonometric system is not an unconditional basis for
L^{p}(\mathbb{T})
whenever
1\leq p\not=2\leq\infty
. The example that we will provide below reveals the subtle cancellation hidden in the Fourier coefficients of a given function and serves as a memento that many of otherwise simple objects when expressed as sums involving Fourier coefficients give rise to only conditionally summable models. Then the challenge in operating with such a model is how to properly regroup the terms (see
e.g.
the case of Littlewood-Paley theory) such that enough cancellation can be captured within the newly formed terms.

However, as we will soon see, sometimes reshaping the entire problem from the frequency to the physical side completely removes this difficult issue of conditional summability. It is this last remark that we would particularly like to emphasise via the example below. Its key underlying philosophy was inspirational for our problem, and, in our search for proving the single scale estimate in Section
5
motivated the idea of substituting the discrete phase-linearized wave-packet model by the continuous phase-linearized spatial model.
89
89

89

For this substitution/transition between the two models one is also invited to consult Section
4.3.2
.

Let
F,G\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
with their Fourier representation given by

F(t)\simeq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n}e^{int}\>\>\>\textrm{and}\>\>\>G(t)\simeq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}b_{k}e^{ikt}.

Then, a simple application of Hölder implies

(144)

FG\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}),

while from the basic properties of the Fourier series we have

(145)

(FG)(t)\simeq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n-k}b_{k})e^{int}.

Taking now
H\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
with
H(t)\simeq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{n}e^{int}
we let

(146)

\Lambda(F,G,H):=((FG)*H)(0)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}F(t)G(t)H(-t)dt.

Now, we conclude that

•

on the one hand, from (
144
), (
145
) and (
146
), we have

\displaystyle\Lambda(F,G,H)\simeq\sum_{n,k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n-k}b_{k}c_{n},

\displaystyle|\Lambda(F,G,H)|\leq\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|H\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}.

•

on the other hand, for generic
\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}=\|G\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}=\|H\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}=1

\sum_{n,k\in\mathbb{Z}}|a_{n-k}||b_{k}||c_{n}|=\infty.

As already hinted earlier, the valuable lesson that we are learning from the above example is that the sums involving (product) of Fourier coefficients (of some functions) are encoding hidden cancellations that can be often exploited by reframing the problem from the Fourier (frequency) setting into a spatial setting.

With this in mind, we return to the high resolution wave-packet discretized model as reflected in (
136
) and notice that we are in a very similar situation: our model may be visualised as a superposition of
product of local Fourier coefficients
attached to
f
and
g
(and with a modified weight for
h
if regarded in the dualized version).

Thus, judging by analogy with the expression in (
145
), we are invited to consider
90
90

90

In this motivational argument we are fixing all the parameters but
u
.
the following elementary block within (
136
):

(147)

\displaystyle S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x):=\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x),

whose joint frequency support regarded from the input side,
i.e.
the support of
(\hat{f},\hat{g})
, is depicted in Figure
6
.

Recalling now (
123
) and (
131
) we further deduce that

(148)

\displaystyle S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\Big(\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle e^{i(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})2u}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})(2\ell-2)}.

v
fixed in the square
{\footnotesize\begin{aligned} &[2^{am-k}(\ell+1),2^{am-k}(\ell+2)]\\
&\times[2^{am-k}(\ell-1),2^{am-k}\ell]\end{aligned}}

Figure 6.
Frequency portrait of the information associated with
S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}
. It is at this level that we manage to exploit the curvature of the phase (see Section
5
) and also obtain the frequency localization of
h\cdot w_{k,n,v}^{e}
(see Section
6
).

Analyzing the right-hand side of (
148
) we deduce the following time-frequency characteristics:

•

in space, thanks to the presence of
\check{\varphi}
, the information is concentrated within
I_{k}^{p_{r}}
;

•

in frequency – see Figure
6
– the joint support in
(\xi,\eta)
encoded within the wave-packets contributing to the sum stays within the strip

\xi-\eta=-2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}2v+O(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}).

Thus, inspecting (
148
) in parallel with (
145
), intuitively we expect

(149)

S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)\approx\frac{1}{2^{k}}e^{2inv}\Big(\int_{I_{k}^{n}}f_{\ell+1}(x-t)g_{\ell-1}(x+t)e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}2vt}dt\Big)\mathbf{1}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x).

The rigorous realization of (
149
) is the subject of our next section.

4.3.2.
Transition from the discrete phase-linearized wave-packet model to the continuous phase-linearized spatial model

In this section we focus on making (
149
) precise. For reasons that will become transparent momentarily, we will reshape the information in (
147
) by isolating the Gabor coefficient of
f
, which we write as:

\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{\ell+1}(s)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{0,p_{r}-n}}(s)e^{-i(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}s-(p_{r}-n))(u-v)}\,ds.

After the change of variable
2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}s-(p_{r}-n)=y
and a periodization argument (similar with the one used in order to prove the Poisson summation formula) this becomes

\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am-2k}{4}}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(\sum_{j_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{\ell+1}\big(\frac{y+j_{1}+(p_{r}-n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(y+j_{1})\Big)e^{-iy(u-v)}dy,

where here
\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}(y)=\check{\varphi}(y)e^{iy2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1)}
.

Applying a similar reasoning for the coefficient
\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle
we can now rewrite (
147
) as

(150)

\displaystyle S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)=\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{0,p_{r}}(x)

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(\sum_{j_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{\ell+1}\big(\frac{y+j_{1}+(p_{r}-n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(y+j_{1})\Big)e^{-iy(u-v)}dy\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(\sum_{j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}g_{\ell-1}\big(\frac{y+j_{2}+(p_{r}+n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(y+j_{2})\Big)e^{-iy(u+v)}dy\Big)e^{i2u(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})}.

Once at this point, observe that we have the following identity: if
a,b\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
, then

(151)

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}}a(y)e^{-iy(u-v)}dy\Big)\cdot\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}}b(y)e^{-iy(u+v)}dy\Big)e^{i2ux}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}a(x-t)b(x+t)e^{-2ivt}dt.

Applying now (
151
) for
a(y):=\sum_{j_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{\ell+1}\big(\frac{y+j_{1}+(p_{r}-n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(y+j_{1})
and
b(y):=\sum_{j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}g_{\ell-1}\big(\frac{y+j_{2}+(p_{r}+n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(y+j_{2})
and inserting the result in (
150
) we deduce

(152)

\displaystyle S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{0,p_{r}}(x)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(f_{\ell+1}\big(\frac{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}-t+j_{1}+(p_{r}-n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}-t+j_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(g_{\ell-1}\big(\frac{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+t+j_{2}+(p_{r}+n)}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+t+j_{2})\Big)e^{-2ivt}dt.

Finally, after applying the change of variable
t\mapsto 2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n
, we conclude

(153)

\displaystyle S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})e^{i(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})2^{\frac{am}{2}}(2\ell-2)}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{I^{n}_{k}}f_{\ell+1}\big(x-t+\frac{j_{1}}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x-t)-p_{r}+n+j_{1})

\displaystyle\cdot g_{\ell-1}\big(x+t+\frac{j_{2}}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x+t)-p_{r}-n+j_{2})e^{-2iv(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n)}\,dt

\displaystyle=:\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{S}_{k,p_{r}}^{n,v}[j_{1},j_{2}](f_{\ell+1},g_{\ell-1})(x).

Observation 4.2
.

Formulation (
153
) ends the rigorous transition from the discrete wave-packet model to the continuous spatial model. As already mentioned, we will make use of it in Section
5
when proving the single estimate (
183
) and then in Section
6
when working at a refined version of (
183
). In all these instances one could work directly with the formulation (
153
) proved above. However, for notational simplicity and fluency of the exposition we will reduce (
153
) to a simpler, approximative form – represented by (
154
) below – and this last formulation will be embraced from now on
in our paper. While we will present an outline of why indeed it is enough to only work with (
154
) we will leave the technical details to the reader.

In light of the above observation, we now provide a brief argumentation for the following claim:

If
x\in I^{p_{r}}_{k}
then without loss of generality one can assume from now on that

(154)

S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)=\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(f_{\ell+1,r},g_{\ell-1,r})(x),

with

(155)

\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(F,G)(x):=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\int_{I^{n}_{k}}F\big(x-t\big)G\big(x+t\big)e^{-2iv(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n)}dt.

and

(156)

\displaystyle f_{\ell+1,r}:=\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{p\sim 2^{am/2}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}\rangle\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}=\sum_{u,p\sim 2^{am/2}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}\rangle\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}},

\displaystyle g_{\ell-1,r}:=\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{p\sim 2^{am/2}}\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}\rangle\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}=\sum_{u,p\sim 2^{am/2}}\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}\rangle\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u,p_{r}}.

Indeed, we first start by noticing that since
\check{\varphi}_{0,1}^{0,0}(y)=\check{\varphi}(y)
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x-t)-p_{r}+n+j_{1})\overline{\check{\varphi}_{0,\ell}^{0,0}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x+t)-p_{r}-n+j_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\check{\varphi}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x-t)-p_{r}+n+j_{1})\overline{\check{\varphi}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x+t)-p_{r}-n+j_{2})

\displaystyle\cdot e^{-i(2(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})+j_{1}+j_{2})2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1)}.

Isolating now the first term in the last line above and ignoring the conjugation we use the information that
\check{\varphi}
is compactly supported in frequency for applying a standard Taylor series argument – here
x\in I^{p_{r}}_{k}
is assumed to be fixed – that has as an effect the tensorization (separation of variables in
x
and
t
):

\displaystyle\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x-t)-p_{r}+n+j_{1})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{i\xi(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}(x-t)-p_{r}+n+j_{1})}\varphi(\xi)d\xi

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\left(i\xi(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+1)\right)^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}!}\frac{\left(-i\xi(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n+1)\right)^{k_{2}}}{k_{2}!}e^{i\xi j_{1}}\varphi(\xi)d\xi

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\left(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+1\right)^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}!}\frac{\left(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n+1\right)^{k_{2}}}{k_{2}!}\check{\varphi}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(j_{1}),

where in the last line
\check{\varphi}_{k_{1},k_{2}}
is a Schwartz function depending on
k_{1},k_{2}
such that
|\check{\varphi}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(j_{1})|\lesssim\frac{C^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}{|j_{1}|^{10}+1}
with
C>0
a suitable absolute constant.

Deduce thus from here that for any
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x\in I^{p_{r}}_{k}
we have

(157)

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{k,p_{r}}^{n,v}[j_{1},j_{2}](f_{\ell+1},g_{\ell-1})(x)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{k}}e^{-i(j_{1}+j_{2})2^{\frac{am}{2}}(\ell-1)}\sum_{{k_{1},l_{1}\in\mathbb{N}}\atop{k_{2},l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}}\frac{\left(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+1\right)^{k_{1}+l_{1}}}{k_{1}!l_{1}!}\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})\check{\varphi}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(j_{1})\check{\varphi}_{l_{1},l_{2}}(j_{2})

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{I^{n}_{k}}f_{\ell+1}\big(x-t+\frac{j_{1}}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)g_{\ell-1}\big(x+t+\frac{j_{2}}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\big)e^{-2iv(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n)}\frac{\left(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n+1\right)^{k_{2}+l_{2}}}{k_{2}!l_{2}!}dt

A simple inspection of (
157
) reveals the following:

•

the presence of the term
\check{\varphi}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(j_{1})\check{\varphi}_{l_{1},l_{2}}(j_{2})
provides a polynomial decay in terms of the size of the parameters
j_{1},j_{2}
thus allowing us to sum in
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
in (
153
);

•

for
x\in I^{p_{r}}_{k}
the expressions
2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+1
and
\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})
are of size
\approx 1
;

•

for
t\in I^{n}_{k}
the expression
2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n+1
is of size
\approx 1
.
91
91

91

It might be instructive to verify – for example – the proof of (
191
) in Section
5
to see why factors such as
\frac{\left(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}t-n+1\right)^{k_{2}+l_{2}}}{k_{2}!l_{2}!}
and
\frac{\left(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r}+1\right)^{k_{1}+l_{1}}}{k_{1}!l_{1}!}
are indeed harmless.

The above explains why, for
x\in I^{p_{r}}_{k}
, the right-hand side in (
153
) can be reduced to the (dominant) term
\tilde{S}_{k,p_{r}}^{n,v}[0,0](f_{\ell+1},g_{\ell-1})
and moreover, why the latter can be further reduced to the expression

\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(f_{\ell+1},g_{\ell-1})
which, given the imposed range for
x
, is well approximated
92
92

92

Strictly speaking
\mathbf{1}_{[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k})}(x)\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(f_{\ell+1},g_{\ell-1})(x)
is a superposition of terms of the form
c_{r-r^{\prime}}\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(f_{\ell+1,r^{\prime}},g_{\ell-1,r^{\prime}})(x)
,
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\{c_{r^{\prime}}\}_{r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{Z})
a fast-decaying sequence obeying
c_{r^{\prime}}=O(|r^{\prime}|^{-2})
as
|r^{\prime}|\rightarrow\infty
.
by
\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(f_{\ell+1,r},g_{\ell-1,r})(x)
, thus certifying (
154
).

4.3.3.
The continuous phase-linearized spatial model revisited – bird’s eye view. The heuristic equivalence of the two high resolution models

This section is meant solely for presenting an alternate, more direct way of visualizing our continuous model that in particular reveals the important role played by the linearization process. Indeed, returning to the original form of our operator
T_{m,k}
we show the very natural way in which one can achieve a decomposition of
T_{m,k}
in elementary blocks resembling the expression in the right-hand side of (
149
). Since the previous section was covered in great detail, the description provided here will be expository, and hence stripped of technicalities.

These being said, let us recall the original formulation

(158)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle=\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\int f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{i\lambda(x)t^{a}}\rho(2^{-k}t)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{2^{k}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\int_{[2^{k},2^{k+1}]}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{i\lambda(x)t^{a}}dt.

Following the same phase linearization approach as the one in Section
4.2.3
with the only distinction that we are now working in space as opposed to frequency there, we first isolate the phase of our kernel

\tau_{x}(t):=\lambda(x)t^{a},

and notice that in the non-trivial regime deduced from (
158
) we must have
|t|\approx 2^{k}
and
|\lambda(x)|\approx 2^{a(m-k)}
. As a consequence, we further have that

\tau_{x}^{\prime\prime}(t)=a(a-1)\lambda(x)t^{a-2}\approx_{a}2^{am-2k},

and hence, in order to focus only on the linear growth around a given point
t_{0}\in[2^{k},2^{k+1}]
one must have the size of the spatial region restricted to
|t-t_{0}|\lesssim 2^{k}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}
.

Thus, recalling that
I^{n}_{k}
stands for
\Big[\frac{n}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}},\frac{n+1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\Big]
, we have that

(159)

T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)\approx\frac{1}{2^{k}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\sum_{n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\int_{I^{n}_{k}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{i\lambda(x)t^{a}}dt.

Given the very construction of
I^{n}_{k}
, taking
t^{n}_{k}:=n2^{k-\frac{am}{2}}
and applying a Taylor series argument around the chosen point
t^{n}_{k}
, we have that

(160)

\tau_{x}(t)=\lambda(x)t^{a}=\lambda(x)(t^{n}_{k})^{a}+(t-t^{n}_{k})a\lambda(x)(t^{n}_{k})^{a-1}+O(1),

as long as
|\lambda(x)|\approx 2^{a(m-k)}
and
t\in I^{n}_{k}
.

Thus, ignoring
93
93

93

This can be done rigorously via a Taylor series argument.
the
O(1)
term appearing in the phase, we have reached
the final form of our (continuous) spatial linearized model which is given by

(161)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)\approx\frac{1}{2^{k}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}e^{i(1-a)\lambda(x)(t^{n}_{k})^{a}}\chi_{I^{p}_{k}}(x)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\int_{I^{n}_{k}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{ia\lambda(x)(t^{n}_{k})^{a-1}t}dt\Big).

We end this section with an informative discussion on how can one visualize the equivalence between the continuous model expressed above in (
161
) and the discretized wave-packet model expressed in (
136
). Starting from the latter and employing definition (
147
) we have

(162)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\sum_{v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x).

Now based on the heuristic in (
149
) we further have

(163)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\sum_{v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{1}{2^{k}}e^{2inv}\left(\int_{I_{k}^{n}}f_{\ell+1}(x-t)g_{\ell-1}(x+t)e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}2vt}dt\right).

Once at this point, relying on the time-frequency correlation as captured by (
140
), we notice that the main contribution is attained when

(164)

-2v\approx\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}},

while from relations (
137
)–(
140
) via a stationary phase argument we deduce

(165)

\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)\approx\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))e^{i(1-\frac{1}{a^{\prime}})\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}}\varphi\big(\xi+\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}\big).

Putting together (
163
), (
164
) and (
165
), at the informal level, we conclude

(166)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\frac{1}{2^{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{a^{\prime}}\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\int_{I_{k}^{n}}f_{\ell+1}(x-t)g_{\ell-1}(x+t)e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}t}dt\Big),

thus certifying for
\bar{c}_{a}=a
the equivalence with the formulation given in (
161
) as claimed.

4.4.
A brief contrasting conclusion regarding the two high resolution models

Our intention here is to summarize the need for utilizing both phase-linearized high-resolution models constructed earlier:

•

On the one hand – see Section
5
, the integral formulation in (
161
) captures enough cancellation to yield the desired single scale version of (
69
), that is

(167)

\|T_{m,k}(f,g)\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim_{a,p,q}2^{-\delta am}\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{q}},

even if one takes absolute values in the summand of (
161
).

As already explained, (
167
) or its dual correspondent cannot be though attained if one ignores the signs of the Gabor coefficients in either (
134
) or in its dual version (
143
), respectively.

•

On the other hand – see Sections
6
and
7
, for upgrading the single scale estimate (
167
) to the global estimate (
69
) one has to involve the interactions among the wave-packet associated with the input functions
f,g
and
h
, thus necessitating the use of the first model operator discussed in Section
4.2
that will now need to be reshaped in order to exploit the BHT-like structure of our operator – this latter key step is part of the design of the low resolution model introduced in the next section.

4.5.
The low resolution model: identifying the underlying BHT structures

In this section we highlight the built-in BHT frame as suggested by the zero-curvature features
(P1)
and
(P2)
and set the scene for the multi-scale time-frequency analysis that will be performed later on in Section
7
. Based on the previous reasonings, we are naturally led to consider two kinds of time-frequency tiling:

•

the first one captures the local, single-scale behavior
94
94

94

As the name suggests, this discretization that is part of the high resolution analysis concerns the local properties of our operator when restricted to a single “big frequency square” – see Figures
2
and
3
. This process has as a result the subdivision of such a big frequency square into smaller, same-scale frequency squares. Of course, each such big frequency square comes with its own family of smaller, uniform-in-scale frequency squares and as a result at the global level we will deal with a multiscale problem whose aim is to quantify the interactions among these families of smaller frequency squares.
of our operator and involves tiles of area
1
; this model is simply the geometric, tile rephrasing of the high resolution wave-packet model discussed in Section
4.2
.

•

the second one accounts for the global, multi-scale behavior of our operator and consists of tiles having area
2^{am}
. This model, referred to as the low resolution wave-packet model, is the expression of the BHT features of
BC^{a}
, and amounts to regrouping the terms within the first model above in order to later exploit the almost orthogonality along multiple scales via tree structures.

4.5.1.
The bilinear Hilbert transform frequency portrait: identifying the structure of the time-frequency regions

The initial decomposition
{1\over t}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rho(2^{-k}t)\over t}
of the singular kernel, followed by the level set decomposition of the phase of the multiplier for the high oscillatory component, and the stationary phase analysis led to the study of the operator
T_{m}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}T_{m,k}
, with each individual term
T_{m,k}
(as defined in (
120
)) having frequency information supported in the region
\{\xi-\eta\sim 2^{am-k}\}
. The subsequent step, designed for independently extracting the frequency information for
f
and
g
, led to considering “big square” frequency regions

(168)

\big[2^{am-k}(\ell+1),2^{am-k}(\ell+2)\big]\times\big[2^{am-k}(\ell-1),2^{am-k}\ell\big].

As
k
and
\ell
vary over the set of integers, this collection of squares identifies with (a part of) a Whitney decomposition of the region
\{\xi>\eta\}
that may be interpreted as a
2^{am}
-dilation of the classical frequency portrait of the bilinear Hilbert transform.
95
95

95

The multiplier associated to BHT is
sgn(\xi-\eta)
, and
\{\xi=\eta\}
represents its singularity set relative to which one performs the above mentioned Whitney decomposition.
Thus, inspired by and in parallel with the standard BHT analysis, we expect the collection induced by the squares in (
168
) to play an analogue role in the analysis of our operator
BC^{a}
.

However, unlike the BHT case, when analyzing the information contained in the above frequency regions (
168
) we will not involve wave-packets adapted to the scale of these big squares. Instead, in order to be able to exploit the curvature component of our
BC^{a}
which is responsible for the strategy presented in Section
2.1
and particularly (
69
), our time-frequency analysis relies on wave-packets adapted to the smaller scale
2^{{am\over 2}-k}
as indicated by the LGC methodology. This produces

(169)

\displaystyle T_{m,k,\ell}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\\
\bar{p}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u,v\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,\bar{p}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,\bar{p}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,\bar{p}}(x)w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle,

which has the frequency information supported in (
168
). In order to control the information contained in the Whitney squares, a certain spatial localization that indicates an (almost) orthogonality relation between objects having the same frequency support is necessary.

Notice that for every
\bar{p}\in\mathbb{Z}
, the spatial information for
f
is adapted to
\Big[\frac{\bar{p}-n}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}},\frac{\bar{p}-n+1}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\Big]
, and that of
g
to
\Big[\frac{\bar{p}+n}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}},\frac{\bar{p}+n+1}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\Big]
. Since
n\sim 2^{am\over 2}
, a
2^{am\over 2}
-separation between the values of
\bar{p}
warrants separation for not only the spatial information for
f
, but also for that of
g
and of the output
T_{m,k,\ell}
. This leads to the regrouping mentioned in (
136
), the definition (
135
) and the representation of
T_{m}
in the form of
T_{m}(f,g)=\sum_{k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)
, where

(170)

\displaystyle T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\\
n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\\
v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x).

The spatial information for
f
relevant to
T_{m,k,\ell,r}
is thus concentrated on

\bigcup_{p,n\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big[\frac{p_{r}-n}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}},\frac{p_{r}-n+1}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\Big],

which can be approximated
96
96

96

Up to considering
O(1)
similar terms.
by
[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]
.
Similarly, the spatial information for
g
is, for the most part, contained in

\bigcup_{p,n\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big[\frac{p_{r}+n}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}},\frac{p_{r}+n+1}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\Big],

and this is also comparable to
[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]
.

In conclusion, for different values of
r
, the spatial information for
f
encoded in
T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)
is located on disjoint intervals; the same holds for
g
and for the output
T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)
. On the other hand, for a fixed
r
, the spatial information for
f
,
g
and the output
T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)
is adapted to the same interval
[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]
.

The regrouping in (
136
), which consisted in gathering
2^{am\over 2}
consecutive integer values of
p
, had two important consequences: (i) the time-frequency information for any of
f,g
or
T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)
featured in
T_{m,k,\ell,r}
, uniquely determines the time-frequency information for the remaining input/output data; (ii) as
\ell
and
r
vary, the time-frequency information is associated to disjoint regions.

So the analogue of the tri-tiles appearing in the BHT analysis are precisely the time-frequency regions of area
2^{am}

(171)

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle:=I_{P}\times\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]\times\big[2^{am-k}(\ell+1),2^{am-k}(\ell+2)\big]

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle:=I_{P}\times\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}}:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]\times\big[2^{am-k}(\ell-1),2^{am-k}\ell\big],

\displaystyle P_{3}

\displaystyle:=I_{P}\times\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{3}}:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]\times\big[2^{am-k}(2\ell),2^{am-k}(2\ell+1)\big],

where
k,\ell,r
vary in
\mathbb{Z}
. We denote
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
the collection of such tri-tiles
P:=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
, in clear analogy to the bilinear Hilbert transform operator:

(172)

{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}:=\{P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})|\text{ with }P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}\text{ given by }\eqref{eq:tri:tiles:def:S4},\text{ for }k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}\}.

Since each of the
P_{j}
has area
2^{am}
, we refer to them as “expanded (tri-)tiles”.

In fact,
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
is characterized (such a collection is sometimes called of
rank 1
) by the following properties:

(i)

the collection of frequency squares
(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\times\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}})_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}
is a Whitney covering of the half-plane
\{\xi>\eta\}
, which means

(173)

\text{dist}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\times\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}},\Gamma)\sim|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}|=|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}}|,

where
\Gamma:=\{\xi=\eta\}
is the boundary of the half-plane;

(ii)

For
P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
, the three tiles
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
have the same spatial support
I_{P}
and are all of area
2^{am}
;

(iii)

For
P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
,
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{3}}\sim\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}+\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}}
, i.e.
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{3}}
is an interval of the same length as
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}}
, from the same dyadic lattice, and contained in
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}+\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}}
.

4.5.2.
Tile-rephrasing of the wave-packet information

In this section we reinterpret the discretization (
170
) of our operator from a geometric point of view by phrasing the action of the wave-packets in terms of tiles.

We start by isolating the wave-packet
\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}
part of the term
\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle
in (
170
). We remark now that its time-frequency portrait is captured within the area one tile

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle:=I^{p_{r}-n}_{k}\times\omega^{u_{\ell}-v}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\Big[(r-1)2^{k}+\frac{p-n}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}},(r-1)2^{k}+\frac{p-n+1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\times\Big[\frac{\ell}{2^{-(am-k)}}+\frac{u-v}{2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}},\frac{\ell}{2^{-(am-k)}}+\frac{u-v+1}{2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}\Big].

Notice now that for
r,k
fixed, as
v,n,p
range over their respective
\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
possible values,
s_{1}
will “span” a time-frequency region which is roughly a
2^{\frac{am}{2}}
-dilate of any other chosen
s_{1}
with similar characteristics, region that can be essentially identified with
P_{1}
from (
171
).

Deduce that, up to a decomposition of the family of parameters into
O(1)
subfamilies, we have
97
97

97

For a better geometric intuition, we refer the reader to Figure
5
in Section
2
.

s_{1}\subset P_{1}

for all
n,v,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
.

Similar considerations hold for
g
as well, and in particular we can introduce

\displaystyle s_{2}

\displaystyle:=I^{p_{r}+n}_{k}\times\omega^{u_{\ell-1}+v}_{k}

\displaystyle=\Big[(r-1)2^{k}+\frac{p+n}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}},(r-1)2^{k}+\frac{p+n+1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\times\Big[\frac{\ell-1}{2^{-(am-k)}}+\frac{u+v}{2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}},\frac{\ell-1}{2^{-(am-k)}}+\frac{u+v+1}{2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}\Big]

so that for
p,n,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
we always have
s_{2}\subset P_{2}
.

Turning towards the wave-packet
\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}
we are also led to introduce

\displaystyle s_{3}

\displaystyle:=I^{p_{r}}_{k}\times\omega^{2u_{2\ell-1}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\Big[(r-1)2^{k}+\frac{p}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}},(r-1)2^{k}+\frac{p+1}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\times\Big[\frac{2\ell-1}{2^{-(am-k)}}+\frac{2u}{2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}},\frac{2\ell-1}{2^{-(am-k)}}+\frac{2u+1}{2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}\Big]

with (again for all
p,n,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}
)
s_{3}\subset P_{3}.

We summarize the roles of the parameters appearing in the definition of the tritiles
s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
,
P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
, and in the model operator (
170
):

•

k
defines the main scale of the decomposition and determines the strip in the frequency plane;

•

the frequency parameter
\ell
indicates the joint frequency localization of
f
and
g
at the scale
2^{am-k}
;

•

the spatial parameter
r
indicates the spatial moral support of the input/output function(s) arising from the action of
T_{m,k,\ell,r}
;

•

the frequency parameters
u
and
v
provide a more accurate localization for
\hat{f}
and
\hat{g}
at the smaller scale
2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}
(this arises from the linearization of the oscillatory phase of the multiplier symbol);

•

the spatial parameter
p
indicates a more accurate spatial information of the output corresponding to the wave-packet
\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}
.

•

the spatial parameter
n
accounts for the spatial localization in the continuous
t
variable (at the same smaller scale
2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}
) exploiting the bilinear Hilbert structure of the input functions that reflects in the
\frac{2n}{2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}}
difference
98
98

98

This is immediate from the spatial structure of the arguments of the input functions:
x+t
for
f
and
x-t
for
g
.
between the moral spatial supports of
f
and
g
, respectively.

We notice that
k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}
are associated with the “expanded tiles”
P
, while the regular
s
tiles of area
1
require also the parameters
p,n,u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}
in order to be located as we are zooming into a given
P
. This explains why we refer to a model involving the tri-tiles
s
as a
high resolution model
and to the model involving the “expanded tiles”
P
as a
low resolution model
.

The expanded tri-tile
P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
and their subordinated tri-tile(s)
s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
are represented in Figure
4
while their inter-relationship is illustrated in Figure
5
. For future reference, we collect all the above definitions :

(174)

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle:=I^{p_{r}-n}_{k}\times\omega^{u_{\ell}-v}_{k},

\displaystyle s_{2}

\displaystyle:=I^{p_{r}+n}_{k}\times\omega^{u_{\ell-1}+v}_{k},

\displaystyle s_{3}

\displaystyle:=I^{p_{r}}_{k}\times\omega^{2u_{2\ell-1}}_{k},

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]\times\big[2^{am-k}(\ell+1),2^{am-k}(\ell+2)\big],

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]\times\big[2^{am-k}(\ell-1),2^{am-k}\ell\big],

\displaystyle P_{3}

\displaystyle:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]\times\big[2^{am-k}(2\ell),2^{am-k}(2\ell+1)\big].

Since later on we will prefer to drop the explicit form of both collections of tri-tiles, we also write for
1\leq j\leq 3
,
s_{j}=I_{s_{j}}\times\omega_{s_{j}}
, where
I_{s_{j}}
is one of the intervals obtained by dividing
I_{P}
into
2^{am\over 2}
pieces of equal length, and
\omega_{s_{j}}
is one of the intervals obtained by dividing
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}
into
2^{am\over 2}
identical pieces.
In such a situation we write

(175)

\hat{s}_{j}=P_{j},\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq 3,\quad\text{or simply }\hat{s}:=P.

More generally we use the notation

(176)

\hat{J}=I

if
J
is obtained by dividing
I
into
2^{am\over 2}
congruent subintervals.

Notice that each of the intervals
I_{P}
is dyadic while each of its high-resolution associate
I_{s_{j}}
obeys
\hat{I}_{s_{j}}=I_{P}
. Correspondingly, each of the intervals
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}
are part of a
2^{am}
dilation of the dyadic grid with
\hat{\omega}_{s_{j}}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}
.

Remark 4.3
.

As mentioned previously,
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
is a rank-1 family: once
P_{i}
determined, so are the other two tiles forming the expanded tri-tiles
P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
and thus only the parameters
k,r,\ell
will be of relevance. In contrast, the collection of tri-tiles
s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
from (
174
) involves the extra parameters
n,p,u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}
, and once
s_{1}
is set, there are still
2^{am\over 2}
choices for the spatial interval
I_{s_{2}}
and
2^{am\over 2}
choices for the frequency interval
\omega_{s_{2}}
in order to fully determine
s_{2}
.
The rank-1 property makes
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
especially suitable for the later multi-scale time-frequency analysis, but, as we will see, we still need to rely on the tri-tiles of area 1 and on their associated wave-packets when estimating the almost orthogonality among the expanded tiles that is quintessential in obtaining the control over
T_{m}
and in extenso over our original operator
BC^{a}
.

Finally, for any
s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
as in (
174
), we let

(177)

\displaystyle\phi_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle:=\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n},\quad

\displaystyle\rho_{I_{P}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x)),

\displaystyle\phi_{s_{2}}

\displaystyle:=\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n},\quad

\displaystyle w^{e}_{s}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),

\displaystyle\phi_{s_{3}}

\displaystyle:=\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}},\quad

\displaystyle|I_{s}|

\displaystyle:=2^{-(\frac{am}{2}-k)}.

4.5.3.
The low-resolution model

With the notations and conventions from above, we are now ready to formally introduce the low-resolution model. This will be delivered in two equivalent forms utilized depending on the context and our convenience.

•

the low resolution model - the parametric format

In this setting, our low resolution model is given by

T(f,g)(x)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}T_{m}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}T_{m,k}(f,g)(x),

with

(178)

\displaystyle T_{m,k}(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)(x),\qquad\text{ where}

\displaystyle T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}},\\
n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}},\\
v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x).

Notice here, that for fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}
the discretization of
T_{m,k,\ell,r}
in (
178
) represents precisely the high-resolution linearized wave-packet discretization obtained in (
136
).

Since we will work extensively with the dualization of these operators, we define the associated trilinear forms by

(179)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}(f,g,h),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle:=\langle T_{m,k}(f,g),h\rangle=\sum_{\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle:=\langle T_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g),h\rangle.

•

the low resolution model - the tile format

Alternatively, our operator can be written in terms of elementary building blocks associated with (expanded) tri-tiles. This format, anticipated in (
76
) of Section
2
, will be used in Section
7
where the time-frequency analysis for the low resolution model will be performed.

In this situation we have

(180)

\displaystyle T(f,g)(x)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}T_{m}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}T_{m,P}(f,g)(x),\qquad\text{ where }

\displaystyle T_{m,P}(f,g)(x):=2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\sum_{s:\hat{s}=P}\frac{1}{|I_{s}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle g,\phi_{s_{2}}\rangle\phi_{s_{3}}(x)\rho_{I_{P}}(\lambda(x))w^{e}_{s}(\lambda)(x).

Here the operators
T_{m,P}
correspond to the operators
T_{m,k,\ell,r}
from the parametrized setting where we assumed that
P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
and
s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
are given by (
174
).

Maintaining the parallelism with the parametric format we also define the associated trilinear forms

(181)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle:=\langle T_{m,P}(f,g),h\rangle.

We end this section with the following

Remark 4.4
.

The low resolution analysis will be critical in Section
7
where we will need to employ time-frequency methods similar in spirit with those used for approaching the bilinear Hilbert transform. While both (
178
) and (
180
) offer a good intuition on the essence of the low resolution format in reality we will need a slight refinement of these formulations that is described in Section
6
.

In what follows we will stick to the parametric format relying on the indices
k,\ell,r,p,n,u,v
as it will be more convenient for performing a high-resolution study of the operator. Once we have completed the finer analysis, we will pass to the tile format. This latter, more geometric description, will be employed along the entire Section
7
.

5.
The high resolution, single-scale analysis (I): Extracting the cancellation encoded in the phase curvature

5.1.
Preparatives

In this section we provide the first key ingredient for the strategy discussed in Section
2
– see in particular (
69
) – that is expressed in the following single scale
m
-exponential decay estimate:
99
99

99

While not needed later in a larger range, it is not difficult to see that this
L^{2}
-based estimate may be extended via interpolation arguments to the full Banach triangle,
i.e.

\|\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m,k}\|_{L^{p}\times L^{q}\times L^{r^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}}\lesssim_{a,p,q,r}2^{-\delta_{0}^{\prime}am}
with
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{r}
and
1<p,q<\infty
and some exponent
\delta_{0}^{\prime}
depending on
p,q
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Fix
k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
and assume
a\in(0,\infty)\setminus\{1,2\}
is a given parameter. With the notations from the previous section we let
100
100

100

While the same as the expression in Section
4.5.3
we restate for convenience the formula of our form here, and also – for only this section – insert the superscript
a
in our formulations in order to stress the dependence of our form as well as of our estimates on this parameter.

(182)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h):=

\displaystyle\sum_{n,v,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}S_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\,w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)\,h(x)\,dx.

Then, there exists
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
absolute constant such that

(183)

|\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)|\lesssim_{a}2^{-\delta_{0}am}\,2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}}.

We start by elaborating on several subtleties pertaining to the above statement and its proof.

We first notice that the conclusion of Proposition
5.1
may be reshaped up to tails
101
101

101

E.g.
a superposition of similar flavor terms with fast decaying coefficients.

(184)

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{a}(f,g,h)|\lesssim_{a}2^{-\delta_{0}am}|I_{k,r}|

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k,r}}|\pi_{\omega_{k,\ell+1}}f|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k,r}}|\pi_{\omega_{k,\ell-1}}g|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k,r}}|h|^{2}\Big)^{1/2},

where here
I_{k,r}:=[r2^{k},(r+1)2^{k}]
,
\omega_{k,\ell+1}:=[2^{am-k}\ell,2^{am-k}(\ell+1)]
,
r,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\pi_{\omega}
stands for the frequency projection operator on the interval
\omega
and
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I}f:=\frac{1}{|I|}\int_{I}f
.

Recalling now that
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{a}(f,g,h)
may be rewritten as
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}^{a}(f,g,h)
with
P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
the tri-tile defined by (
174
), we notice that (
184
) would correspond to a
single tile
estimate if the function
h
would be well-localized in time-frequency.

A more thorough inspection reveals now two key features of (
184
):

(a)

it is
asymmetric
in the time-frequency localization of the inputs
(f,g,h)
: indeed, the functions
f
and
g
are well adapted to the expected time-frequency regions represented by the expanded tiles
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
respectively; however, for the function
h
our estimate only provides the spatial localization corresponding to
P_{3}
;

(b)

it is a
purely
L^{2}
-based estimate that focuses on the norm
\|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}^{a}\|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}\times L^{2}\to\mathbb{C}}
(and thus, in particular, goes beyond the usual Hölder duality range).

As expected, these two features strongly relate with both our proof methodology and the structure of our paper:

(I)

as per item
(a)
above, the shape of the single scale estimate in (
184
) indicates that its proof favors the
bilinear
behavior
(f,g)
within the structure of
T_{m,k,\ell,r}^{a}
treating the third function
h
as an auxiliary object. This is further on reinforced by the item
(b)
which hints that (
184
) involves a
TT^{*}
argument in disguise, since
\|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{a}\|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}\times L^{2}\to\mathbb{C}}=\|T_{m,k,\ell,r}^{a}\|_{L^{2}\times L^{2}\to L^{2}}
.

(II)

the lack of a good time-frequency localization for the function
h
observed in
(a)
hints towards the fact that one needs a further refinement of the single scale estimate (
184
) in order to develop the multiscale analysis required for providing the full boundedness range for our original operator
BC^{a}
. Indeed, as mentioned in the introduction, the treatment of
BC^{a}
has to include the approach for the classical BHT with the latter relying fundamentally on the geometry/combinatorics of the (expanded) tri-tiles
P=(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
and hence on the good localization of the dualizing function
h
. Also the observation in
(b)
will offer the right setting for constructing and adapting suitable notions of sizes for each of the functions
f,g,\text{ and }h
consistent with the multilinear time-frequency analysis for BHT.

Based on the information provided in Sections
4.1
,
4.2.2
and
4.4
in what follows we will briefly recap and provide more context for

(i)

the features discussed at
(a)
and
(b)
and juxtaposed to
(I)
and
(II)
,

(ii)

the necessity of going back and forth between the discrete linearized wave-packet (no
m
-decay absolute summability) model and the continuous linearized spatial (
m
-decay absolute summability) model.
102
102

102

It might be worth noticing that this is a direct consequence of the rough behavior of the linearizing function
\lambda(x)
; indeed, if
\lambda(x)
is smooth (say e.g. polynomial in
x
) then one can work directly with the discrete phase-linearized wave-packet model which obeys the
m
-decay absolute summability property.

Indeed, as already revealed in Section
4
, our initial approach is guided by the frequency discretization of our operator consistent with the phase linearization of the multiplier (see Section
4.2.3
) followed by an adapted Gabor frame discretization of the input functions
f
and
g
. This achieves the discretization of our dualized form
\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m,k}(f,g,h)
as given by (
84
) and hence completes the first two steps (L) and (G) in our LGC-methodology described in Section
4.2.2
. However, the attempt of completing our program with the final step (C) faces a key difficulty: the wave-packet coefficients appearing in the discretized form
\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m}(f,g,h)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m,k}(f,g,h)
do not produce an
m
-decaying absolute summable model. As already explained, we thus have to make a detour and reverse the Gabor discretization reshaping the linearization at the first step onto the spatial variable (kernel side) – see Sections
4.3.2
and
4.3.3
and compare this with Section
5.2
. The spatial linearization offers a critical advantage in that it circumvents the problem of the conditional
m
-decaying summability for
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{a}(f,g,h)
(see Appendix
A
) since now, via (
182
), the signum of the local Fourier (Gabor) coefficients within (
147
) are encrypted through Parseval, into a bilinear oscillatory integral of the form (
154
)–(
155
). Once at this point we pass to the (C) item exposed in Section
4.2.2
and employ what one might refer to as a
bilinear
TT^{*}
-argument
that relies on exponential sums
103
103

103

The fact that our treatment of a continuous nature problem is based on discrete analysis elements such as exponential sums is quite surprising; in more standard situations one usually sees the reverse applied - with continuous realm serving as a model and offering assistance to discrete analysis. In our setting, the specific shape of these exponential sums is dictated by the spatial phase linearization discretization.
and on level set estimates exploiting the time-frequency correlation and the non-zero curvature of the phase. This provides the insight to our proof of (
184
), which gives us the desired exponential decay in
m
but, as mentioned at item (a), without also providing a good time-frequency localization for the function
h
. The latter aspect however is necessary when passing from the single scale (fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}
) to the multiscale analysis (summation over
k\in\mathbb{Z}
). This is why we need a refinement of the single scale estimate that is performed in Section
6
and that – via the transition explained in Section
4.3.2
– combines the features of both the present approach via spatial linearization and that of the initial linearized wave-packet discretized model ending up by providing a single scale estimate with both exponential decay in
m
and well localized information for the triple
(f,g,h)
.

5.2.
Transition from the discrete phase-linearized wave-packet model to the continuous phase-linearized spatial model

This section can be thought as an adaptation of the reasonings presented in Section
4.3.2
to the specific context provided by Proposition
5.1
.

We start our reduction with the following simple observation: since our estimate (
183
) is invariant under
translation, modulation and dilation symmetries, it is enough to only treat the case
k=0
,
\ell=1
and
r=1
(and hence
p_{r}=p
). Next, for notational simplicity, throughout this section we will refer to the wave-packets
\check{\varphi}^{u,n}_{\frac{am}{2},1}
as simply

\check{\varphi}^{u,n}
.

Recalling now (
182
), an immediate application of Cauchy-Schwarz gives

(185)

|\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{a}_{m,0,1,1}(f,g,h)|\lesssim\Lambda_{m}^{a}(f,g)\,\|h\|_{L^{2}},

where here we used the notation
104
104

104

Throughout the paper
\bar{c}_{a}
is an absolute nonzero constant that is allowed to change from line to line. Also, throughout this section we set
\langle x\rangle:=(1+x^{2})^{1/2}
.

(186)

\displaystyle[\Lambda_{m}^{a}(f,g)]^{2}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho_{am}(\lambda(x))}{2^{\frac{am}{4}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,v\\
\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Big|\sum\limits_{u,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\langle f,\check{\varphi}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle\langle g,\check{\varphi}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle\check{\varphi}^{2u,p}(x)\Big|}{\big\langle\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}+2v\big\rangle^{2}}\Bigg)^{2}\,dx.

From (
185
), (
186
) and the symmetry invariance discussed earlier, we deduce that (
183
) is an immediate consequence of

(187)

|\Lambda_{m}^{a}(f,g)|\lesssim_{a}2^{-\delta_{0}am}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}}.

Notice now that since (
186
) is a positive expression, from now on we can assume without loss of generality that

(188)

\rho_{am}(\lambda(x))=1\qquad\textrm{for any $x\in\mathbb{R}$}.

Once at this point, we remark – via (
149
) (or the more precise (
154
)) – that our desired estimate (
187
) follows if we are able to prove a similar bound for the continuous version of our bilinear form
\Lambda_{m}^{a}(f,g)
, as given by
105
105

105

Here, as before,
I^{n}=[n\,2^{-\frac{am}{2}},\,(n+1)\,2^{-\frac{am}{2}}]
.
:

(189)

\displaystyle[\underline{\Lambda}_{m}^{a}(f,g)]^{2}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\int_{I^{p}}\Bigg(\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\,\frac{\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}}\,2vt}\,dt\Big|}{\big\langle\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)+2v\big\rangle^{2}}\Bigg)^{2}\,dx.

Observation 5.2
.

In formulation (
189
) above, for clarity purposes, we used a standard almost-orthogonality argument in
p
in order to only focus on the main diagonal term and discard the error term produced by the “tails” arising from the wave-packet localization. We will not provide here an argumentation for this reduction since there are several similar arguments carried over in detail in Sections
4
and
6
.

We now claim that the behavior of
\underline{\Lambda}_{m}^{a}(f,g)
may be reduced to that of
106
106

106

Here is where we use the time-frequency correlation provided by the expression appearing at the denominator in the right-hand side of (
189
).

(190)

\displaystyle[\bar{\Lambda}_{m}^{a}(f,g)]^{2}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\int_{I^{p}}\bigg(\sum_{n\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{i\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a(a-1)m}{2}}}\lambda(x)\,t}\,dt\Big|\bigg)^{2}\,dx.

Indeed, recalling the notation
M_{b}g(x):=e^{-ibx}g(x)
,
b\in\mathbb{R}
, we further claim that

(191)

\underline{\Lambda}_{m}^{a}(f,g)\lesssim\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}:|s|\leq 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}_{m}^{a}(f,M_{2^{\frac{am}{2}}s}g)}{s^{2}+1}.

This last statement is based on a rather standard technical reasoning involving a Taylor series argument. However, for reader’s convenience, we provide below a sketch of its proof: we first notice that

\displaystyle[\underline{\Lambda}_{m}^{a}(f,g)]^{2}\approx\sum_{p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\int_{I^{p}}\bigg(\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\,\sum_{|s|\leq 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\Big(\Big|\frac{\bar{c}_{a}\,n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)+2v-s\Big|\lesssim 1\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}}\,2vt}\,dt\Big|}{s^{2}+1}\bigg)^{2}\,dx.

Fix now
x
, and assume
\Big|\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)+2v-s\Big|\lesssim 1
. Isolating the
t
-integrand, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}}\,2vt}\,dt\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}}st}e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}}\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)t}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0002pt\cdot e^{-i2^{\frac{am}{2}}(t-(n-1)2^{-\frac{am}{2}})(\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)+2v-s)}\,dt\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Big(\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)+2v-s\Big)^{k}}{k!}\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)(M_{2^{\frac{am}{2}}s}g)(x+t)

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0002pt\cdot e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}}\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2}}}\lambda(x)t}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}(t-(n-1)2^{-\frac{am}{2}}))^{k}\,dt\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big|\int_{I^{n}}f(x-t)(M_{2^{\frac{am}{2}}s}g)(x+t)e^{i\frac{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a-1}}{2^{\frac{a(a-1)m}{2}}}\lambda(x)t}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0002pt\cdot\frac{(2^{\frac{am}{2}+10}(t-(n-1)2^{-\frac{am}{2}}))^{k}}{k!}\,dt\Big|.

Now, since
2^{\frac{am}{2}+10}\,(t-(n-1)\,2^{-\frac{am}{2}})
is a function that is morally constant one for
t\in I^{n}
we conclude that (
191
) holds.

With these done, based on the above considerations, the proof of (
183
) is an immediate consequence of Proposition
5.3
below.

5.3.
The continuous phase-linearized spatial model formulation of the single scale estimate

In this section we formulate the single scale estimate in the desired continuous phase-linearized spatial form and perform some simple reductions involving the distribution of the spatial information carried by the input functions
f
and
g
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
a\in(0,\infty)\setminus\{1,2\}
be a fixed parameter and
\lambda:\mathbb{R}\to[2^{am-10},2^{am+10}]
be an arbitrary Lebesgue measurable function.

Then there exists
107
107

107

At the end of this proof we provide an explicit value for
\delta_{0}
though this is far from optimal. One can indeed increase the value of the exponent
\delta_{0}
but we prefer to trade off this improvement over the clarity of the exposition since for our purposes any non-trivial decay (i.e
\delta_{0}>0
) is good enough.
an absolute constant
\delta_{0}>0
such that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, one has

(192)
