) thus clarifying our claim.
∎

Observation 6.2
.

Before proceeding to analyze the consequences of (
333
), we digress briefly to reassure the reader that it will indeed be sufficient to consider only the
\nu=\nu^{\prime}=0
case of the operators above.
Observe that since
2u=(u-v)+(u+v)
, the expression
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p^{\prime},\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
in the proof of Lemma
6.1
becomes a linear combination of expressions similar to the initial
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}
: indeed, this can be expressed as

\displaystyle\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}(f,g)(x)=\sum_{\vartheta=0}^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}{\nu+\nu^{\prime}\choose\vartheta}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\sum_{u\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\big\langle f_{\ell+1},\Big(\frac{u-v}{2^{am\over 2}}\Big)^{\vartheta}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\big\langle g_{\ell-1},\Big(\frac{u+v}{2^{am\over 2}}\Big)^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-\vartheta}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\big\rangle\,(i2^{am-k}(x-\underline{t}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}))^{\nu^{\prime}}e^{i(2^{{am\over 2}-k}x-p_{r})2u}.

In this context it is important to stress that we are only going to need
L^{2}\times L^{2}\to L^{2}
bounds for these quantities which therefore are completely determined by the
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-sum of the Gabor coefficients of the input functions. As in Subsection
4.3.2
then, we can replace each expression

\displaystyle\sum_{u\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\big\langle f_{\ell+1},\Big(\frac{u-v}{2^{am\over 2}}\Big)^{\vartheta}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\big\rangle\,\big\langle g_{\ell-1},\Big(\frac{u+v}{2^{am\over 2}}\Big)^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-\vartheta}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\big\rangle e^{i(2^{{am\over 2}-k}x-p_{r})2u}

with the corresponding continuous one as in (
154
), where now the frequency projected functions
f_{\ell+1,r},g_{\ell-1,r}
are replaced by the slight variants

(336)

\displaystyle f_{\ell+1,r}^{\vartheta}(x)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\cdot 2^{am\over 2}<u_{1}\leq 3\cdot 2^{am\over 2},\\
n_{1}-2^{am\over 2}r\sim 2^{am\over 2}\end{subarray}}\big\langle f_{\ell+1},\Big(\frac{u_{1}}{2^{am\over 2}}\Big)^{\vartheta}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},n_{1}}\big\rangle\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},n_{1}}(x),

(337)

\displaystyle g_{\ell-1,r}^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-\vartheta}(x)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq u_{2}\leq 2^{am\over 2},\\
n_{2}-2^{am\over 2}r\sim 2^{am\over 2}\end{subarray}}\big\langle g_{\ell-1},\Big(\frac{u_{2}}{2^{am\over 2}}\Big)^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-\vartheta}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},n_{2}}\big\rangle\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},n_{2}}(x).

Clearly these are minor modifications of the projections. We point out that the expressions above contain another abuse of notation: the wave-packets outside the inner products in the last display are themselves slight modifications of the standard ones, obtained by multiplication on the spatial side by
(-i2^{am-k}(x-\underline{t}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}))^{\nu^{\prime}}
. This factor is always bounded by
2^{\nu^{\prime}}
on
\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}
. As
|u_{1}|
and
|u_{2}|
are both bounded by
3\cdot 2^{am/2}
, neither the frequency properties of the wave-packets nor their fast decay away from
I_{k}^{n_{1}}
are going to be affected by the modification: the whole analysis – and in particular the energy estimate that will be proven in Proposition
7.12
– remains valid for these modified operators.

As

\sum_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}\geq 0}\frac{1}{\nu!}\frac{1}{\nu^{\prime}!}\sum_{\vartheta=0}^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}{\nu+\nu^{\prime}\choose\vartheta}2^{\nu^{\prime}}3^{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}

is absolutely summable, it will indeed be enough to consider the case
\nu=\nu^{\prime}=\vartheta=0
, that is, (
333
).

Once at this point it is worth noticing the following feature that later will be used extensively: in (
333
) (and thus also later in (
339
)) we have the flexibility of allowing
w
to depend on the interval
\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}
and on any of the parameters
p^{\prime},v,n
, as well as on the shift parameter
j
.

Using this and putting now together (
328
)–(
333
), we deduce that for any sequence of positive
L^{\infty}
-weights
132
132

132

Here for notational simplicity we only keep the
j
dependence although based on the earlier comment one may allow for each
w_{j}=w_{j}(m,k,\ell,r,p^{\prime})
.

\{w_{j}\}_{j}
not identically zero on any
\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}
, we have that – up to superposing in the right-hand side similar terms with fast decaying amplitudes – the following holds:

(338)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{j}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)\approx\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,v,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim_{\lfloor\rfloor_{p}}2^{am}}|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\langle\underline{H}_{k,r}^{n,v,p}\big(x+\frac{j}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}}\big),\tilde{\varphi}_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\,\frac{\displaystyle\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)w_{j}(x)\,dx}{\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{j}(x)\,dx}\>.

It now remains to notice that by applying Cauchy-Schwarz we obtain via (
331
) that for fixed parameters
k,\ell,r
,

(339)

\displaystyle\big|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)\big|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}\frac{\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1,j}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|}{\big(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{j}(x)\,dx\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}\big|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)\big|^{2}w_{j}(x)\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Above
\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1,j}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}
is a modified version of
\tilde{\varphi}_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}(\cdot-j2^{k-{am\over 2}})
as given by

(340)

\displaystyle\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1,j}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}(x)

\displaystyle:=2^{\frac{am-k}{2}}e^{i2^{\frac{am}{2}}(2^{\frac{am}{2}-k}x-p_{r})(2\ell-2)}

\displaystyle\cdot\check{\psi}\big(2^{am-k}(x-\underline{t}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}-\frac{j}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}})\big)\,\check{\varphi}(2^{{am\over 2}-k}x-p_{r})\,,

where we recall that
p=\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-{am\over 2}}\rfloor
.

Due to the well-localized spatial properties of
\check{\psi}\big(2^{am-k}(\cdot-\underline{t}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}-\frac{j}{2^{{am\over 2}-k}})\big)
(supported in
\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}+j2^{am\over 2}|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|
) and
\check{\varphi}(2^{{am\over 2}-k}\cdot-p_{r})
(adapted to the interval
I_{k}^{p_{r}}
), the functions
(\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1,j}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}})_{j}
satisfy the same properties (in both space and frequency) with an overall amplitude decaying faster than any negative power of
1+|j|
. Hence it is enough to focus on the term
j=0
. We invite the interested reader to consult Remarks
6.8
and
6.9
for a more detailed account on the modifications required in order to treat the terms corresponding to the
j\neq 0
case. In this context, for notational simplicity, we will continue to refer to
\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)
as simply
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)
.

We conclude this section by noticing that via (
339
) we achieved our declared aim – that of providing a good time-frequency localization for
h
(relative to a proper weight) while bringing
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)
to a form that is amenable to the one-scale analysis of Section
5
with special stress on Corollary
5.6
.

6.3.
Splitting the trilinear form according to mass and input distribution

We start this section by recalling that for the proof of the single scale estimate in Section
5
it was important to split the analysis into cases, according to whether the size
133
133

133

This was quantified in terms of a parameter
\mu\in(0,1)
– see the proofs of Propositions
5.5
and
5.3
.
distribution of the inputs was uniform or clustered. It should thus come as no surprise that these concepts will continue to be relevant here. We will further refine that analysis by introducing a new concept, that of
mass
– see Remark
6.4
below.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
\delta_{1},\mu\in(0,1)
be as in Section
5
and assume
k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{Z}
fixed. Let
f
and
g
be Schwartz functions, with
f_{\ell+1,r},g_{\ell-1,r}
as given in (
156
). We then partition the set of all possible
(p^{\prime},n,v)
indices into:

•

the set of
light mass
indices

(341)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{l}:=\bigg\{{{(p^{\prime},n,v)}\atop{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am},\;n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}}\;:\;\frac{1}{|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\,dx<2^{-\delta_{1}am}\bigg\};

•

the set of
heavy mass - uniform
(f,g)
-distribution
indices

(342)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{u}:=\bigg\{{{(p^{\prime},n,v)}\atop{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am},\;n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}}\;:\;\frac{1}{|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\,dx\geq 2^{-\delta_{1}am},

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k}^{\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\rfloor_{r}-n}}|f_{\ell+1,r}|^{2}\leq 2^{\mu\frac{am}{2}-k}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\text{ and }\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k}^{\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\rfloor_{r}+n}}|g_{\ell-1,r}|^{2}\leq 2^{\mu\frac{am}{2}-k}\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigg\};

•

the set of
heavy mass - clustered
(f,g)
-distribution
indices

(343)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}:=\bigg\{{{(p^{\prime},n,v)}\atop{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am},\;n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}}\;:\;\frac{1}{|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\,dx\geq 2^{-\delta_{1}am},

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k}^{\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\rfloor_{r}-n}}|f_{\ell+1,r}|^{2}>2^{\mu\frac{am}{2}-k}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\text{ or }\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k}^{\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\rfloor_{r}+n}}|g_{\ell-1,r}|^{2}>2^{\mu\frac{am}{2}-k}\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigg\}.

Of course, the sets of indices
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{c}
depend implicitly on
f,g,k,\ell,r
but, for simplicity, we leave this dependency out of the notation.

Remark 6.4
.

i) A careful inspection of the above definition reveals that, even if not introduced formally, the concept of mass associated with the indices
(p^{\prime},n,v)
and defined by

(344)

\frac{1}{|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\,dx,

plays an important role in our analysis. This should not be surprising since the concept of mass captures the amount of information relative to
\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}
carried by the linearizing stopping time function
\lambda(x)
that appears in the maximally modulated kernel of
BC^{a}
. This can be seen as an artifact inherited from the Carleson type behavior of our operator that ressembles the mass analysis of the classical Carleson operator introduced by Fefferman in
[
37
]
.

ii) It will be useful later on to use a larger set
\bar{\mathfrak{c}}
with
\mathfrak{c}\subset\bar{\mathfrak{c}}:=\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}\times\{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}\}
, which does not contain joint information in
n
and
v
:

(345)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}:=\bigg\{{{(p^{\prime},n)}\atop{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am},n\sim 2^{am\over 2}}}\;:\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k}^{\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\rfloor_{r}-n}}|f_{\ell+1,r}|^{2}>2^{\mu\frac{am}{2}-k}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\text{ or }\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I_{k}^{\lfloor p^{\prime}2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\rfloor_{r}+n}}|g_{\ell-1,r}|^{2}>2^{\mu\frac{am}{2}-k}\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigg\}.

Now notice that for any
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}
fixed, there are at most
O(2^{(1-\mu)\frac{am}{2}})
values of
n
such that
(p^{\prime},n)\in\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}
.

With these steps completed, the trilinear form (
328
) splits into three terms according to the partition introduced in Definition
6.3
:

(346)

\displaystyle\big|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}}\ldots+\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\ldots+\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\ldots

\displaystyle=:\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{l}}(f,g,h)+\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{u}}(f,g,h)+\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{c}}(f,g,h).

6.4.
Estimates for the localized trilinear forms
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{l}},\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{u}},\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{c}}

In this section we will further reshape the information carried within each of the three forms in decomposition (
346
). This will streamline the modulation invariant multiscale analysis performed in the next section.
134
134

134

Recall that we restrict here our attention to the main term
j=0
in the trilinear form and that for notational simplicity we refer to
\underline{\mathcal{L}}^{0}_{m,k,\ell,r}
as simply
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}
. For more on the general case,
i.e.
arbitrary values for
j
, please see Remarks
6.8
and
6.9
.

6.4.1.
Preliminary bounds

We begin by applying (
339
) to each of the terms in (
346
). For each case we will make use of the above mentioned freedom in choosing our weight
w
.

1.

The
\mathfrak{l}
-component

In order to estimate
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{l}}(f,g,h)
, we apply (
339
) with the constant weights
w=1
; this choice is hinted by the smallness of the mass parameter paired with the trivial bound guaranteed by Proposition
6.5
below. With these, an application of Cauchy-Schwarz yields

(347)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{l}}(f,g,h)\lesssim\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}\big|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)\big|^{2}dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

2.

The
\mathfrak{u}
-component

For
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{u}}(f,g,h)
, we use the weights
w=w_{k,n,v}
. This choice is dictated by the setting provided by Corollary
5.6
, that will be required in order to bound with exponential
m
-decay the expression
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{u}}
.

Now, using definition (
342
) of
\mathfrak{u}
, we have

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w(x)\,dx=\frac{1}{|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\,dx\gtrsim 2^{-\delta_{1}am},

and hence, applying again Cauchy-Schwarz, we deduce

(348)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{u}}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{\delta_{1}am}{2}}\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}\big|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)\big|^{2}w_{k,n,v}(x)dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

3.

The
\mathfrak{c}
-component

Lastly, motivated by the clustered component’s low cardinality – see ii) in Remark
6.4
, we estimate
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{c}}(f,g,h)
in the same manner as
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{l}}
, that is by taking
w=1
and applying Cauchy-Schwarz:

(349)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{c}}(f,g,h)\lesssim\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}\big|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)\big|^{2}dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

6.4.2.
Bounds of
L^{2}\times L^{2}\to L^{2}
type for
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p^{\prime}}(f,g)

It is now necessary to estimate the
\ell^{2}
quantities involving
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p^{\prime}}(f,g)
. For the
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{c}
cases we use the trivial bound provided by Plancherel that is nevertheless sufficient for our purposes.

Proposition 6.5
.

For fixed parameters
k,\ell,r
, one has

(350)

\displaystyle\Big(

\displaystyle\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)|^{2}\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{am}{4}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\Big(\sum_{u_{1}\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{n_{1}-2^{am\over 2}r\sim 2^{am\over 2}}|\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},n_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sum_{u_{2}\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{n_{2}-2^{am\over 2}r\sim 2^{am\over 2}}|\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},n_{2}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{am}{4}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

The estimate above is a direct consequence of the orthogonality of the wave-packets involved in the definition of
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}
– see (
320
). Indeed, squaring the left-hand side in the first inequality of (
350
) and rearranging the summation, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\int_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)(x)|^{2}\,dx.

Since
\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,p}(f,g)
is a periodic function of period
|I_{k}^{p_{r}}|
, Plancherel equality implies that the quantity above is equal to

\displaystyle 2^{-2k}\,2^{-({am\over 2}-k)}\,\sum_{n,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{u\sim 2^{am\over 2}}|\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle|^{2}\,|\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{-\frac{am}{2}}2^{-k}\Big(\sum_{u_{1}\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{n_{1}-2^{am\over 2}r\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big|\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},n_{1}}\rangle\Big|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\cdot\Big(\sum_{u_{2}\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{n_{2}-2^{am\over 2}r\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big|\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},n_{2}}\rangle\Big|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\approx 2^{-\frac{am}{2}}2^{-k}\,\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

∎

For the
\mathfrak{u}
case we argue that the treatment of the corresponding uniform component in Proposition
5.5
– and more precisely in Corollary
5.6
– carries forward to our situation in (
348
). It yields the following:

Proposition 6.6
.

For
k,\ell,r
fixed and
\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}
as defined in (
155
), we have

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}|\tilde{S}_{k}^{n,v}(F,G)(x)|^{2}

\displaystyle w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)dx\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}\,a\,m}2^{-\frac{am}{4}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|G\|_{L^{2}}.

From the above Proposition
6.6
and (
154
) we immediately deduce

Corollary 6.7
.

For
k,\ell,r
fixed, we have

(351)

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}}|\underline{S}_{k,\ell,r}^{n,v,\big\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2^{am\over 2}}\big\rfloor}(f,g)(x)|^{2}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)dx\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}\,a\,m}2^{-\frac{am}{4}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}.

As a final remark, notice that the estimate (
351
) – corresponding to the heavy mass uniform case
\mathfrak{u}
– is an improvement over the rougher bound in (
350
) due to the presence of the extra decaying factor
2^{-\delta_{1}\,am}
.

6.4.3.
Final form of the estimates

Putting together all the information collected so far (using (
350
) for
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{c}
and (
351
) for
\mathfrak{u}
), we obtain that:

(352)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{l}}(f,g,h)\lesssim 2^{-\frac{am}{4}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\,\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 50.00008pt\cdot\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\Big)^{1/2},\end{aligned}

(353)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{u}}(f,g,h)\lesssim 2^{-\frac{\delta_{1}am}{2}}2^{-\frac{am}{4}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\,\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 50.00008pt\cdot\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\Big)^{1/2},\end{aligned}

(354)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{c}}(f,g,h)\lesssim 2^{-\frac{am}{2}}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|f_{\ell+1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\,\|g_{\ell-1,r}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\hskip 50.00008pt\cdot\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.\end{aligned}

Estimates (
352
), (
353
) and (
354
) are single-scale estimates with a sharp time-frequency localization for all three functions
f,g,h
, as desired. It will remain to incorporate them into the BHT time-frequency analysis (the low resolution model), which will be the subject of the next section. With that in mind, it will be convenient to switch to the tri-tiles notation introduced in Section
4.5
. In particular, we refer the reader to (
174
) for a dictionary that relates the indices
(k,\ell,r)
to the time-frequency regions
P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
.

We end this section with the following remarks.

Remark 6.8
.

As we pointed out previously, in the two previous sections we have focused only on the main term corresponding to
j=0
. Let us briefly explain the modifications that need to be performed in order to deal with an arbitrary value of the shift
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. This adaptation essentially requires a “translation” of the previous analysis from the interval
{\underline{I}}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}
to the shifted interval

{\underline{I}}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime},(j)}:={\underline{I}}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}+j2^{am\over 2}|{\underline{I}}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|.

•

The mass is then defined by

\frac{1}{|\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime}}|}\int_{\underline{I}_{k,r}^{p^{\prime},(j)}}w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\,dx.

•

Proposition
6.6
and Corollary
6.7
are still valid with the only modification of replacing the weight
w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)
by its shifted version
w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)(\cdot+j2^{k-{am\over 2}})
. Such an operation has no effect on the different proofs. In particular, shifting the weight is equivalent to shifting the phase function
\lambda
and since the estimates in the proof of Corollary
5.6
do not depend on the stopping time
\lambda
, we can safely apply them.
135
135

135

Indeed, we stress that the parameter
\delta_{1}
and the definition of “uniform” distribution for
f,g
involving the parameter
\mu
do not depend on the stopping time
\lambda
.

•

Everything will bring us to similar estimates as (
352
)-(
354

Remark 6.9
.

(W2)

are compactly supported

Besides the pointwise decay illustrated in

7.
The low resolution, multi-scale analysis: modulation invariance analysis

With the single scale estimate simultaneously featuring bounds with exponential decay in
m
and securing well-localized time-frequency information for all the three functions
f,g
, and
h
of the associated trilinear form, we are finally set to perform the multi-scale time-frequency analysis characteristic to multilinear operators having the frequency portrait of BHT. This will allow us to prove Theorem
3.3
, from which Theorems
3.1
and
1.2
follow.

With
a\in(0,\infty)\setminus\{1,2\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
fixed for the reminder of the section, we will proceed as follows:

•

the single-scale estimates of Section
6.4.3
will be reformulated in terms of tri-tiles, leading to the study of certain trilinear forms
\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{*}(f,g,h)
associated to the rank-1 family
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
;

•

to treat these trilinear forms, we adapt classical time-frequency methods to our context. In particular, in our situation both the rank-1 collection
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
of expanded tri-tiles of area
2^{am}
and the finer collection of tri-tiles
\{s:\hat{s}\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}\}
of area
1
will be involved in this analysis;

•

as a consequence of the above we will deduce the boundedness of
T_{m}
in the strict local-
L^{2}
range (
2<p,q,r^{\prime}<\infty
);

•

finally, using a suitable interpolation procedure – which consists in localizing the trilinear form information in space and interpolating between maximal sizes – we extend the boundedness of
T_{m}
to the range of Hölder exponents with
2<p,q\leq\infty
and
1<r<\infty
– thus proving Theorem
3.3
.

7.1.
Reformulation of the estimates in Section
6.4.3
in terms of
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
tri-tiles

In the next subsections we will focus on proving Proposition
7.2
. Before this though, we continue with some notions of bilinear time-frequency analysis.

7.2.
Preliminaries on time-frequency tools

In our approach we will use as a black box the results in
[
82
]
. This will require nevertheless the introduction of several adjusted notions as well as some non-trivial adaptations to the present context.

The trilinear forms
\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{\ast}(f,g,h)
are defined with respect to the rank-1 family of tri-tiles
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
. The key geometric observation in this context is that – loosely speaking – (pairwise) overlapping of a collection of tiles
\{P_{1}\}_{P\in\mathbb{P}}
implies (pairwise) disjointness for each of the associated collections
\{P_{2}\}_{P\in\mathbb{P}}
and
\{P_{3}\}_{P\in\mathbb{P}}
. This insight naturally leads to the notion of tree – an object of lower complexity (visualized as a multiplier singular at a point) that can be easily controlled. The strategy introduced in
[
63
]
,
[
64
]
consists in a greedy algorithm that selects “orthogonal” trees according to how much information they store (the size of the tree) and to certain geometric conditions. Next, one needs to put together this information, for which estimating the spatial supports of the trees associated to a fixed size becomes important (this leads to the notion of energy associated to a collection of tri-tiles).

In order to take advantage of the orthogonality among the tree structures, it becomes convenient to assume a suitable sparseness within the collection
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
: a separation of the scales (whenever
P,P^{\prime}\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
are so that
|I_{P^{\prime}}|<|I_{P}|
, we should have
|I_{P^{\prime}}|\ll|I_{P}|
) and a separation of the Whitney cubes from (
173
) at a given scale. This sparseness, which will be implicit in the present section, is achieved by splitting
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
into
O(1)
similar collections. Moreover, standard limiting arguments allow us to assume that
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
is finite, and hence also that the collection of trees is finite.

Definition 7.4
.

For
1\leq j\leq 3
, we call a collection of tri-tiles
\mathbf{T}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
a
j
-lacunary
tree
with top data
(I_{\mathbf{T}},(\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{1}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{2}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{3}}))
if

\forall\,P\in\mathbf{T},\qquad\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{j}}\in 3\,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}\setminus\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}\quad\text{and}\quad I_{P}\subseteq I_{\mathbf{T}_{j}}.

In contrast, a collection of tri-tiles
\mathbf{T}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
is called a
j
-overlapping
tree
with top data
(I_{\mathbf{T}},(\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{1}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{2}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{3}}))
if

\forall\,P\in\mathbf{T},\qquad\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{j}}\in\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}\quad\text{and}\quad I_{P}\subseteq I_{\mathbf{T}_{j}}.

We call
tree
with top data
(I_{\mathbf{T}},(\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{1}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{2}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{3}}))
any collection
\mathbf{T}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
which is a
j
-lacunary or
j
-overlapping tree for some
1\leq j\leq 3
.

The Whitney property of the collection
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\times\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{2}}\}_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}
(see (
173
)) ensures that a
j
-overlapping tree is lacunary in the remaining two directions. So we can assume that the tree top information
(\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{1}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{2}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}_{3}})
is located in the set
\{(\xi,\xi,2\xi):\xi\in\mathbb{R}\}
.

Definition 7.5
.

Two
j
-lacunary trees
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
are said to be
strongly disjoint
if either
\displaystyle 3\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}\cap 3\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{j}}=\emptyset
for all
P\in\mathbf{T},P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
or

(369)

\Big[3\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}\cap 3\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{j}}\neq\emptyset\textrm{ and }|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P}|\ll|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}}|\Big]\text{ implies }I_{P^{\prime}_{j}}\cap 3\,I_{\mathbf{T}}=\emptyset.

Remark 7.6
.

We emphasize that our notion of strong disjointness is not the standard one from
[
82
]
that reads as

(370)

\Big[3\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{j}}\cap 3\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{j}}\neq\emptyset\textrm{ and }|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P}|\ll|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}}|\Big]\text{ implies }I_{P^{\prime}_{j}}\cap\,I_{\mathbf{T}}=\emptyset.

However, (
369
) and (
370
) are achieved in a similar fashion – see also Remark
7.10
.

The extra separation of the trees required in our definition will be helpful in the proof of the energy estimate (
389
).

Now, following
[
82
]
, we can introduce the concepts of
size
and
energy
:

Definition 7.7

(Size)
.

For
\mathbb{P}\subseteq{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
and
(a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}}
a sequence of complex numbers, we define for
j=1,2,3

(371)

\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{j}((a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}}):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{T}\subset\mathbb{P}\\
\mathbf{T}\,j-\text{lacunary tree}\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{1}{|I_{T}|}\sum_{P\in T}|a_{P_{j}}|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Definition 7.8

(Energy)
.

For
\mathbb{P}
a subcollection of
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
and
(a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}}
a sequence of complex numbers, we define for
j=1,2,3

(372)

\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{j}((a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}}):=\sup_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sup_{\mathscr{F}}2^{d}\Big(\sum_{\bf T\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|\Big)^{1/2}

where
\mathscr{F}
ranges over all collections of strongly disjoint
j
-lacunary trees
\mathbf{T}\subset\mathbb{P}
with the property that

\Big(\frac{1}{|I_{\mathbf{T}}|}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}|a_{P_{j}}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}>2^{d}

and for any subtree
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{T}

\Big(\frac{1}{|I_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}|}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}|a_{P_{j}}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq 2^{d+1}.

With the above notations and definitions we have the following general estimate:

Proposition 7.9

(Similar to Proposition 6.5 of
[
82
]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{P}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
be a finite collection of tri-tiles and
(a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}}
a sequence of complex numbers. Then for any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3}\in[0,1)
with
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}+\theta_{3}=1

(373)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}}\frac{1}{|I_{P}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}a_{P_{1}}a_{P_{2}}a_{P_{3}}\Big|\lesssim\prod_{j=1}^{3}\big(\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{j}((a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}})\big)^{\theta_{j}}\big(\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{j}((a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}})\big)^{1-\theta_{j}}.

Remark 7.10
.

Only small modifications are needed in order for the proof of
[
82
, Proposition 6.5]
to extend to the present context which requires the more rigid strong disjointness property (
369
).

The reader familiar with
[
82
]
or
[
80
]
will recognize that the strong disjointness property (
370
) is intimately related to the greedy algorithm mentioned in the beginning of the section. Indeed, given
\mathbb{P}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
a collection of tri-tiles such that
\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{j}((a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}})\leq 2^{d}
, one starts by selecting maximal trees
\mathbf{T}\subset\mathbb{P}
with
\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbf{T}}_{j}((a_{P_{j}})_{P\in\mathbb{P}})>2^{d-1}
satisfying also a certain geometric condition; in order to ensure that (
370
) holds for
j
-lacunary trees (interacting with
\mathbf{T}
) that will be selected later, one discards also
{\mathbf{T}}^{\circ}
, the
j
-overlapping tree having the same top data
(I_{\mathbf{T}},\xi_{\mathbf{T}})
as
\mathbf{T}
.

In our situation, once the
j
-lacunary tree
\mathbf{T}
is selected by the algorithm, we discard not only
{\mathbf{T}}^{\circ}
as above, but also the
j
-overlapping trees
{\mathbf{T}}^{+}
having top data
(I_{\mathbf{T}}+|I_{\mathbf{T}}|,\xi_{\mathbf{T}})
and
{\mathbf{T}}^{-}
having top data
(I_{\mathbf{T}}-|I_{\mathbf{T}}|,\xi_{\mathbf{T}})
(i.e. the spatial intervals of
{\mathbf{T}}^{+}
and
{\mathbf{T}}^{-}
consist in the right/left neighbour of
I_{\mathbf{T}}
). This, in short, guarantees that (
369
) is satisfied within the greedy algorithm used for proving (
373
).

Proposition
7.9
above will be applied directly to the study of
\Lambda^{*}_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}(f,g,h)
for
*\in\{\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{u}\}
with

a_{P_{1}}:=f(P_{1}),\qquad a_{P_{2}}:=g(P_{2})\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad a_{P_{3}}:=h_{*}(P_{3}),

thus providing the control of these trilinear forms in terms of sizes and energies of
f,g,h
.

In the next subsections, we will obtain bounds for such sizes and energies, which will then allow us to conclude the desired estimates in Section
7.5.1
.

7.3.
Estimates for sizes

First we address the size estimates, which will be naturally
141
141

141

For standard tiles of area 1, the sizes emerge as
L^{1}
quantities, i.e. the maximal
L^{2}
averages in the right-hand side of (
375
) can be replaced by maximal
L^{1}
averages.
bounded by
L^{2}
maximal averages, associated to trees. Since the tree top data
I_{\mathbf{T}}
is not intrinsically associated to tiles in
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
whilst the information associated to
\mathbf{T}
is spatially adapted to
I_{\mathbf{T}}
, we define for any
\mathbb{P}
finite subcollection of
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
the collection
\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}
of spatial intervals

(374)

\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}:=\{I\text{ dyadic }:I=I_{P}\text{ for some }P\in\mathbb{P}\text{ or }I=I_{\mathbf{T}}\text{ for some tree }\mathbf{T}\subset\mathbb{P}\}.

Proposition 7.11

(Size estimates)
.

Let
\delta_{1},\mu>0
be as obtained in Section
5
and as used in Definition
6.3
when introducing the indices sets
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{c}
corresponding to each tile
P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
.

For any locally
L^{2}
-integrable functions
f,g,h
and any subcollection
\mathbb{P}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
, we have

(375)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{1}((f(P_{1}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{2}((g(P_{2}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}},

and

(376)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}((h_{\mathfrak{l}}(P_{3}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{\delta_{1}am}{2}}

\displaystyle\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}},

(377)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}((h_{\mathfrak{u}}(P_{3}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}},

(378)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}((h_{\mathfrak{c}}(P_{3}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{\mu am}{4}}

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}.

In the above proposition, only estimates (
376
)-(
378
) require some work; the
L^{2}
-based (
375
) is standard and follows from the almost orthogonality of the wave-packets. For completeness, we treat all of them.

Proof of estimate (
375
).

We let
\mathbf{T}
be a
1
-lacunary tree and we aim to prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\big|^{2}\lesssim\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

When
f
is supported close to
I_{\mathbf{T}}
, this is a simple consequence of orthogonality; on the contrary, for
f
supported away from
I_{\mathbf{T}}
we rely on the fast decay of the wave-packets.

Assume that
f
is supported on
3I_{\mathbf{T}}
; then it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\big|^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This follows from the disjointness of the collection of tiles
\displaystyle\{s_{1}\}_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}
(a weak consequence of the fact that
\mathbf{T}
is
1
-lacunary) and from the almost orthogonality of the associated collection of wave-packets
\displaystyle\{\phi_{s_{1}}\}_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}
.

Next we assume that
f
is supported in
I_{\mathbf{T}}^{\nu}:=I_{\mathbf{T}}+\nu|I_{\mathbf{T}}|
, for
|\nu|\geq 2
an integer, and we want to prove

(379)

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\big|^{2}\lesssim|\nu|^{-10}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

First, we notice that the left-hand side of the estimate above writes as

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}\\
|I_{s_{1}}|=2^{-{am\over 2}}2^{-\kappa}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|\end{subarray}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\big|^{2},

and also

\displaystyle\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\int_{I_{\mathbf{T}}^{\nu}}\frac{1}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{s_{1}},x)}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big)^{-20}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\int_{I_{\mathbf{T}}^{\nu}}\frac{1}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big(\frac{\text{dist}(I_{s_{1}},x)}{2^{-{am\over 2}}2^{-\kappa}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|}\Big)^{-20}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>\frac{2^{am\over 2}2^{\kappa}}{|I_{\mathbf{T}}|}\int_{I_{\mathbf{T}}^{\nu}}2^{-10am}2^{-20\kappa}\big(\frac{\text{dist}(I_{\mathbf{T}},x)}{|I_{\mathbf{T}}|}\big)^{-20}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,2^{-9am}2^{-10\kappa}|\nu|^{-10}.

For a fixed scale
\kappa\geq 0
, there are at most
2^{\kappa}\cdot 2^{am}
tiles
s
so that
\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}
, with
|I_{s_{1}}|=2^{-{am\over 2}}2^{-\kappa}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|
. This rough estimate is enough for concluding (
379
); by standard arguments, (
375
) follows.
∎

Notice that the estimates for the light-mass and the heavy-mass/clustered sizes
\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{l}}(P_{3}))
and
\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{c}}(P_{3}))
feature additional decay in the parameter
m
. First we will prove estimate (
377
), for which we will not make use of the fact that we are summing over triples
(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}
. Next, we prove (
378
), in which the additional decay is a result of the fact that for any fixed
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}
, only a small fraction of the allowed values of
n
is such that
(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}
(see Remark
6.4
). Lastly, for (
376
), the decay comes from the assumption that the indices
(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}
are associated to light averages of the weight
w_{|P|,n,v}(\lambda)
.

Proof of estimate (
377
).

Let
\mathbf{T}
be a
3
-lacunary tree; it will be enough to prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{\mathfrak{u}}(P_{3})^{2}\lesssim\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By definition (
363
), this amounts to proving

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\lesssim\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

We will see that orthogonality presents itself in disjointness across different scales, because of the presence of the stopping time
\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(\cdot))
. First, by using Cauchy-Schwarz and properties
0(WP1)
-
0(WP2)
, one has

(380)

\displaystyle\big|\langle h\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\int|h(x)|^{2}\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))^{2}|w_{|P|,n,v}(\lambda)(x)|^{2}|\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}(x)|\,dx\Big)\cdot\Big(\int|\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}(x)|\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\int|h(x)|^{2}\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))^{2}|w_{|P|,n,v}(\lambda)(x)|^{2}|\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}|^{-{1\over 2}}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\,dx\Big)|\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}|^{1\over 2}

and our desired estimate reduces to

(381)

\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{u}}\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\|h\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda)w_{|P|,n,v}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}})}^{2}\lesssim\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Using (
140
) to bound
w_{|P|,n,v}(\lambda)
and extending the summation to all admissible triples
(p^{\prime},n,v)
we have

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{\mathfrak{u}}(P_{3})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\sum_{(n,v,p^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{u}}\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\|h\,\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{k,n,v}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h(x)|^{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\sim 2^{am\over 2}\\
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\Big)dx.

We claim that the following pointwise bound holds:

(382)

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\sim 2^{am\over 2}\\
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}\end{subarray}}\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\lesssim\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}(x).

If the above is true then this will be enough to conclude (
381
), since the left/right-hand side in (
382
) is independent of
n
(notice that the sum over
n\sim 2^{am/2}
is compensated by the factor of
2^{-am/2}
).

To see the claim, first notice that after summing in
v
the left-hand side of (
382
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x).

For every
x\in\mathbb{R}
, there are only
O(1)
many
k\in\mathbb{Z}
for which
\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\neq 0
, and for every such
k

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x)\lesssim\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}(x),

which allows us to conclude (
382
).
∎

Proof of estimate (
378
).

The overall argument will be similar to the previous one. We start again with
\mathbf{T}
, a
3
-lacunary tree, and observe that it will be enough to prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{\mathfrak{c}}(P_{3})^{2}\lesssim 2^{-\mu{am\over 2}}\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

As before, we have

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{\mathfrak{c}}(P_{3})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h(x)|^{2}\Big(\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\Big)dx.

The desired estimate will be a consequence of the pointwise bound

(383)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\frac{\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\mu\frac{am}{2}}\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}(x),

which in turn follows from

(384)

\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{c}}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\frac{1}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}\lesssim 2^{-\mu\frac{am}{2}}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x),

for any fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
P\in\mathbf{T}
.

In Remark
6.4
, we introduced a larger set
\bar{\mathfrak{c}}=\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}\times\{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}\}
containing
\mathfrak{c}
which allowed us to separate the
n
and
v
parameters; thus the left-hand side in the expression above is bounded by

(385)

\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n)\in\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}\sum_{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\frac{1}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}.

With
p^{\prime}
and
n
fixed, we first sum in
v
to get

(386)

\sum_{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\frac{1}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{4}}\lesssim 1.

Now we can bound (
385
) by

\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n)\in\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}\sum_{n:(p^{\prime},n)\in\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x).

We recall the observation in Remark
6.4
: once
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}
is fixed, there are at most
O(2^{(1-\mu)\frac{am}{2}})
values of
n
so that
(p^{\prime},n)\in\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}
. That produces as upper bound for (
385
)

\frac{1}{2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}2^{(1-\mu)\frac{am}{2}}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x),

which is of course bounded by the right-hand side of (
384
).

Estimate (
383
) follows immediately from (
384
) and the straightforward pointwise estimate

(387)

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbf{T}:\\
|I_{P}|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x)\lesssim\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}(x).

∎

Proof of estimate (
376
).

In this situation, taking
\mathbf{T}
a
3
-lacunary tree, our task is to show that

(388)

\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{\mathfrak{l}}(P_{3})^{2}\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}am}\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{\mathbf{T}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

We will use the fact that for a fixed tri-tile
P
, we are summing over the indices
(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}
in the “light-mass regime”. Indeed, by Cauchy-Schwarz and
0(WP3)
we can bound

\displaystyle\big|\langle h\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda)w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|h^{2}\rho_{|P|}(\lambda)^{2}w_{|P|,n,v}|\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}|\big\|_{L^{1}}\;\big\|w_{k,n,v}|\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}|\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}am}\big\|h^{2}\rho_{|P|}(\lambda)^{2}w_{|P|,n,v}\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}\big\|_{L^{1}}

(this is the natural “weighted” replacement of (
380
)). For any
P\in\mathbf{T}
we then have

\displaystyle\qquad h_{\mathfrak{l}}(P_{3})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}am}\frac{1}{2^{\frac{a}{2}m}}\sum_{(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h(x)|^{2}\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))^{2}w_{|P|,n,v}(\lambda)(x)\tilde{\chi}_{\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}am}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h(x)|^{2}\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x)dx.

Estimate (
388
) follows now by essentially the same reasoning used for (
381
) since the information
(p^{\prime},n,v)\in\mathfrak{l}
is no longer needed.
∎

7.4.
Estimates for energies

Now we turn towards the energies, which are naturally
L^{2}
-based quantities as they capture the almost orthogonality of strongly disjoint trees associated to a fixed size level.

Proposition 7.12

(Energy estimates)
.

For any
L^{2}
functions
f,g,h
and any subcollection
\mathbb{P}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
, we have

(389)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{1}((f(P_{1}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{2}((g(P_{2}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}}

and for
\ast\in\{\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{c}\}

(390)

\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}((h_{\ast}(P_{3}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}})\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}}.

Remark 7.13
.

We point out that in establishing the energy estimates (
390
) we will not make use of the partitioning of the triples
(p^{\prime},n,v)
into subsets
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{c}
. This partition was only important for the size estimates of Proposition
7.11
.

Proof of estimate (
390
).

Let
\ast
stand for any of the
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{u}
or
\mathfrak{c}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a collection of mutually disjoint
3
-lacunary trees satisfying for some integer
d

2^{2d}<\frac{1}{|I_{\mathbf{T}}|}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{*}(P_{3})^{2}\qquad\text{for all }\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}.

It is then enough to show that

(391)

\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}h_{*}(P_{3})^{2}\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which will in turn be a direct consequence
142
142

142

Recall that the factor
2^{-am/2}
in the definition of
h_{\ast}(P_{3})
allows one to safely sum in
n\sim 2^{am/2}
.
of the estimate, uniform in the parameter
n\sim 2^{am\over 2}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\sim 2^{am\over 2}\\
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}\end{subarray}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Psi_{P_{3}}^{p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

There are two key observations to make:

(i)

Given
I
a dyadic interval, we denote by
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{F}}(I)
the collection of frequency intervals
\boldsymbol{\omega}
for which
I\times\boldsymbol{\omega}
corresponds to a
P_{3}
tile:

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{F}}(I):=\{\boldsymbol{\omega}:|I||\boldsymbol{\omega}|=2^{am},I\times\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\bigcup_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\bigcup_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\{P_{3}\}\}.

Then notice that
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{F}}(I)
consists of mutually disjoint intervals of the same length.

(ii)

w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}
and
\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}
do not depend on the frequency interval of the tile
P
but only depend on the spatial interval
I_{P}
and on the triple of parameters
(p^{\prime},n,v)
.

Hence, for any fixed interval
I
with
|I|=2^{k}
we can use property
0(WP4)
for the collection
\big\{\Psi_{I\times\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{p^{\prime}}\big\}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{F}}(I),p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}}
and function
F:=h\,\rho_{I}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{I,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)
to deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{F}}(I)}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\sim 2^{\frac{a}{2}m},\\
p^{\prime}\sim 2^{am}\end{subarray}}\big|\langle h\,\rho_{I}(\lambda(\cdot))w_{I,n,v}^{e}(\lambda),\Psi_{I\times\boldsymbol{\omega}}^{p^{\prime}}\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{v\sim 2^{\frac{a}{2}m}}\|h\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda)w_{k,n,v}\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Estimate (
391
) follows once we notice that for every fixed
n
(and uniformly so)

\sum_{k}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathscr{D}:\\
|I|=2^{k}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{v\sim 2^{\frac{a}{2}m}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}(x)^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)\lesssim 1.

This follows from (
386
) and an unlocalized version of (
387
).
∎

It remains to prove energy estimate (
389
), which relies on more subtle orthogonality properties. Although similar to the classical argument for BHT (see
[
82
, Lemma 6.6]
), we will

•

rely on the extra separation condition in (
369
);

•

use the smaller tri-tiles
s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
of area 1 (to which wave-packets are naturally adapted), in order to capture the orthogonality between the strongly disjoint trees through a
TT^{*}
argument.

Proof of estimate (
389
).

By symmetry, we consider only the energy for
f
. Fix an integer
d\in\mathbb{Z}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a collection of strongly disjoint
1
-lacunary trees having the property that for any
\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}

(392)

2^{d}<\Big(\frac{1}{|I_{\mathbf{T}}|}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

and

(393)

\Big(\frac{1}{|I_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}|}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 2^{d+1}\quad\text{for any subtree }\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{T}.

It will suffice to prove

(394)

2^{2d}\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which, based on (
392
) and a standard
TT^{*}
argument, follows from

(395)

\Big\|\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\phi_{s_{1}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim 2^{2d}\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|.

Expanding the square, it suffices to prove

(396)

\sum_{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|\lesssim 2^{2d}\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|.

Now notice that in order for
\displaystyle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle
to be non-zero it must be necessarily that
\omega_{s_{1}}\cap\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\neq\emptyset
and hence one of them is included in the other.
143
143

143

This property is inherited from the parent collection
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\}_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}
, which forms a lattice.

It is thus enough to prove the following two claims:

•

Claim
1
:

(397)

\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{s:\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|\lesssim 2^{2d}\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|;

•

Claim
2
:

(398)

\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}\subset\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\\
|\omega_{s_{1}}|\ll|\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}|\end{subarray}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|\lesssim 2^{2d}\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|.

We start with the proof of Claim 1. First, by symmetry,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\big\rangle|^{2}\big|\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\big\rangle|^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{s_{1}},I_{s^{\prime}_{1}})}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big)^{-10}.

Now, for
\omega_{s_{1}}
fixed, the tiles
s^{\prime}
such that
\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\cup_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
having the property that
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}
are mutually disjoint and satisfy
|I_{s^{\prime}_{1}}|=|I_{s_{1}}|
; hence

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{s_{1}},I_{s^{\prime}_{1}})}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big)^{-10}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\int_{I_{s^{\prime}_{1}}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{s_{1}},x)}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big)^{-10}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{s_{1}},x)}{|I_{s_{1}}|}\Big)^{-10}dx\lesssim 1.

In combination with (
393
) this implies Claim 1, since we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\\
\omega_{s_{1}}=\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\hat{s}\in\mathbf{T}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\big\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{2d}\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}|I_{\mathbf{T}}|.

Let us then focus on the second claim, which will be more technical as interactions between tiles of different frequency scales will need to be counted in. First, note that the left-hand side of (
398
) can be written as

(399)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}=P\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{s}^{\prime}=P^{\prime},\\
\omega_{s_{1}}\subsetneq\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}\,\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\big|

and by Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

(400)

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{ \hat s'=P'}{\omega_{s_1} \subsetneq\omega_{s'_1}}}\big|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|\leq

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{\hat{s}=P}|\langle f,\phi_{s_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\big(\sum_{\hat{s}^{\prime}=P^{\prime}}|\langle f,\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{ \hat s'=P'}{\omega_{s_1} \subsetneq\omega_{s'_1}}}|\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The subtree condition (
393
) applied to the singleton subtrees
\{P\}\subset\mathbf{T}
and
\{P^{\prime}\}\subset\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
provides the following upper bound for (
399
):

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}2^{2d+2}|I_{P}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|I_{P^{\prime}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{ \hat s'=P'}{\omega_{s_1} \subsetneq\omega_{s'_1}}}|\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The anticipated estimate (
396
) follows if we prove, for any tree
\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}
, the inequality

(401)

\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}|I_{P}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|I_{P^{\prime}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{ \hat s'=P'}{\omega_{s_1} \subsetneq\omega_{s'_1}}}|\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim|I_{\mathbf{T}}|.

Before moving on, we take a closer look at the geometry of the tiles in
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
.

(i)

if
P\in\mathbf{T}
and
P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
with
\mathbf{T},\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}
, and
s,s^{\prime}
are so that
\hat{s}=P,\hat{s}^{\prime}=P^{\prime}
with
\omega_{s_{1}}\subsetneq\omega_{s^{\prime}_{1}}
then

•

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\subsetneq\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}
and the sparseness of the collection
{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
implies that
|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}|\ll|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}|
and
|I_{P}|\gg|I_{P^{\prime}}|
;

•

the trees
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
cannot coincide: due to their
1
-lacunarity, tiles of different scales belonging to the same tree cannot overlap in frequency;

•

since
\mathscr{F}
consists of strongly disjoint
1
-lacunary trees, we must have

(402)

I_{P^{\prime}}\cap 3I_{\mathbf{T}}=\emptyset.

(ii)

notice that once
\mathbf{T}\in\mathscr{F}
and
P\in\mathbb{P}
are fixed, the spatial supports of the tiles
P^{\prime}
(contained in any of the trees
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}
) having the property that
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\subsetneq\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}
and
|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}|\ll|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}|
, are mutually disjoint – otherwise such tiles would intersect both in space and in frequency and the strong disjointness property would be disobeyed.

We return to understanding

(403)

\Big(\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{ \hat s'=P'}{\omega_{s_1} \subsetneq\omega_{s'_1}}}|\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
P\in\mathbf{T},P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
are such that
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\subsetneq\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}},|I_{P}|\gg|I_{P^{\prime}}|
and
I_{P^{\prime}}\cap 3I_{P}\neq\emptyset
. This quantity measures the correlation between the tiles
P
and
P^{\prime}
, as perceived
144
144

144

If the wave-packets
\phi_{s_{1}}
were supported both in space and frequency on the tile
s_{1}
, then (
403
) would amount to
0
.
through the wave-packets
\displaystyle\{\phi_{s_{1}}\}_{\hat{s}=P},\{\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\}_{\hat{s}^{\prime}=P^{\prime}}
.

Our claim is that

(404)

\Big(\sum_{\hat{s}=P}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\hat s'=P'}{\omega_{s_1} \subsetneq\omega_{s'_1}}}|\langle\phi_{s_{1}},\phi_{s^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{|I_{P^{\prime}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|I_{P}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},I_{P^{\prime}})}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-5}.

Before proving this estimate, we show how it allows to conclude (
401
) and consequently (
398
). Using (
404
), the left-hand side of (
401
) is bounded by

\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\\
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\subsetneq\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}|I_{P^{\prime}}|\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},I_{P^{\prime}})}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-5}.

For
P\in\mathbf{T}
fixed, we invoke
(ii)
above, to deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\sum_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\\
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\subsetneq\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}\end{subarray}}|I_{P^{\prime}}|\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},I_{P^{\prime}})}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}\int_{(3I_{\mathbf{T}})^{C}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},x)}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-5}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathbf{T}}|I_{P}|\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},(3I_{\mathbf{T}})^{C})}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-3}

\displaystyle\lesssim|I_{\mathbf{T}}|.

Now we return to proving (
404
) for
P
and
P^{\prime}
fixed, as above. This inequality will be itself a consequence of

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{I}=I_{P}}\sum_{\hat{I^{\prime}}=I_{P^{\prime}}}\sum_{\hat{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}}\sum_{\hat{\omega}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}}|\langle\phi_{I\times\omega},\phi_{I^{\prime}\times\omega^{\prime}}\rangle|^{2}\lesssim\frac{|I_{P^{\prime}}|}{|I_{P}|}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},I_{P^{\prime}})}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-10}.

Here we will mostly rely on the spatial decay: given that
|I_{P}|\gg|I_{P^{\prime}}|
,
|I|=2^{-{am\over 2}}|I_{P}|
and
|I^{\prime}|=2^{-{am\over 2}}|I_{P^{\prime}}|
, we have

|\langle\phi_{I\times\omega},\phi_{I^{\prime}\times\omega^{\prime}}\rangle|^{2}\lesssim\frac{|I^{\prime}|}{|I|}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I,I^{\prime})}{|I|}\Big)^{-50}=\frac{|I_{P^{\prime}}|}{|I_{P}|}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I,I^{\prime})}{|I|}\Big)^{-50}.

For fixed intervals
I
and
I^{\prime}
with
\hat{I}=I_{P}
and
\hat{I^{\prime}}=I_{P^{\prime}}
, there are precisely
2^{am\over 2}
intervals
\omega
and
2^{am\over 2}
intervals
\omega^{\prime}
so that
\hat{I}\times\hat{\omega}=P
and
\hat{I^{\prime}}\times\hat{\omega^{\prime}}=P^{\prime}
respectively. This implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{I}=I_{P}}\sum_{\hat{I^{\prime}}=I_{P^{\prime}}}\sum_{\hat{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}}\sum_{\hat{\omega}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}}|\langle\phi_{I\times\omega},\phi_{I^{\prime}\times\omega^{\prime}}\rangle|^{2}\lesssim 2^{am}\frac{|I_{P^{\prime}}|}{|I_{P}|}\sum_{\hat{I}=I_{P}}\sum_{\hat{I^{\prime}}=I_{P^{\prime}}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I,I^{\prime})}{|I|}\Big)^{-50}.

We focus on the last part. When
I
and
I^{\prime}
are so that
\hat{I}=I_{P}
and
\hat{I^{\prime}}=I_{P^{\prime}}
, with
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}\subsetneq\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}
, the separation condition (
402
) has as a consequence

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{I}=I_{P}}\Big(1+\frac{\text{dist}(I,I^{\prime})}{|I|}\Big)^{-50}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},I^{\prime})}{2^{-{am\over 2}}|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-40}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-20am}\frac{1}{|I^{\prime}|}\int_{I^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},x)}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-40}dx.

Now we sum in
I^{\prime}
to get

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{I}=I_{P}}\sum_{\hat{I^{\prime}}=I_{P^{\prime}}}\sum_{\hat{\omega}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P_{1}}}\sum_{\hat{\omega}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}}|\langle\phi_{I\times\omega},\phi_{I^{\prime}\times\omega^{\prime}}\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-10am}\frac{|I_{P^{\prime}}|}{|I_{P}|}\frac{1}{|I_{P^{\prime}}|}\int_{I_{P^{\prime}}}\Big(\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},x)}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-40}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-10am}\frac{|I_{P^{\prime}}|}{|I_{P}|}\Big(\frac{\text{dist}(I_{P},I_{P^{\prime}})}{|I_{P}|}\Big)^{-40}.

This proves (
404
), finishing thus our proof.
∎

Remark 7.14
.

The size estimates (
376
)–(
378
) continue to hold, with an extra
(1+|j|)^{-{M\over 2}}
decay, when we factor in the
j
-shifts discussed in Remark
6.9
. Indeed, although the wave-packets
\Phi_{am-k,2\ell-1,j}^{2^{am}(r-1)+p^{\prime}}
take into account the information in
\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}+j2^{am\over 2}|\underline{I}_{P}^{p^{\prime}}|
– thus supported within
I_{P}+j|I_{P}|
– thanks to the decaying factor
(1+|j|)^{-{M}}
we can regard it as adapted to
I_{P}
through the bump function
\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}
.

The same reasoning applies to the energy estimate (
390
), and further, allows to deduce the same localized estimate (
409
) when taking the shifts into account.

The size and energy estimates together with Proposition
7.9
allow us to prove Proposition
7.2
.

7.5.
Establishing the boundedness range for
T

Thanks to the estimates worked in subsections
7.2
-
7.4
we first establish the
m
-decay boundedness of
T_{m}
within the strictly local-
L^{2}
case (
2<p,q,r^{\prime}<\infty
). This is the content of Corollary
7.3
which follows from Proposition
7.2
via decomposition (
368
). Notice that, due to the summability in
m
, the same boundedness range is implied for
T
.

Next, with Proposition
7.2
settled, we present a refinement of the above time-frequency analysis, which, based on an intermediate interpolation step executed at a localized level, implies (
m
-exponential decaying) estimates for
T_{m}
in the wider range
\{2<p,q\leq\infty\ ;\ 1<r<\infty\}
thus proving Theorem
3.3
. Finally, as it was explained in Section
3.4
, the full range of Theorem
3.1
follows subsequently from standard interpolation techniques.

7.5.1.
Boundedness of
T
in the local-
L^{2}
range

As mentioned above, this is an immediate consequence of Corollary
7.3
, which, in turn, follows from Proposition
7.2
. Thus our focus will be on proving the latter.

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

We first establish restricted-type estimates: for any finite measure sets
E_{1},E_{2},E_{3}\subset\mathbb{R}
and any triple of functions
f,g,h
satisfying

|f(x)|\leq\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}}(x)\text{ a.e.},\quad|g(x)|\leq\mathbf{1}_{E_{2}}(x)\text{ a.e.},\quad|h(x)|\leq\mathbf{1}_{E_{3}}(x)\text{ a.e.},\quad

we claim to have

(405)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{\mathfrak{u}}(f,g,h)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|E_{1}|^{\frac{1}{p}}\,|E_{2}|^{\frac{1}{q}}\,|E_{3}|^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}},

(406)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{\mathfrak{l}}(f,g,h)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{2}^{\prime}am}

\displaystyle|E_{1}|^{\frac{1}{p}}\,|E_{2}|^{\frac{1}{q}}\,|E_{3}|^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}},

(407)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{\mathfrak{c}}(f,g,h)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{2}^{\prime}am}

\displaystyle|E_{1}|^{\frac{1}{p}}\,|E_{2}|^{\frac{1}{q}}\,|E_{3}|^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}},

for any
p,q,r^{\prime}\in(2,\infty)
satisfying
\frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}
and some exponent
\delta_{2}^{\prime}:=\delta_{2}^{\prime}(p,q)>0
.

We start with (
405
). Estimates (
375
) and (
377
) imply for
\mathbb{P}={{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}

\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(f(P_{1}))\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{2}(g(P_{2}))\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{u}}(P_{3}))\lesssim 1;

similarly, due to (
389
) and (
390
),

\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(f(P_{1}))\lesssim|E_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{2}(g(P_{2}))\lesssim|E_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{u}}(P_{3}))\lesssim|E_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Applying Proposition
7.9
with the size/energy estimates and the choice
\theta_{1}=1-\frac{2}{p}\in(0,1),\theta_{2}=1-\frac{2}{q}\in(0,1)
and
\theta_{3}=-1+\frac{2}{r}\in(0,1)
, one has

\displaystyle\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{\mathfrak{u}}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle\lesssim|E_{1}|^{{1-\theta_{1}}\over 2}\cdot|E_{2}|^{{1-\theta_{2}}\over 2}\cdot|E_{3}|^{{1-\theta_{3}}\over 2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|E_{1}|^{1\over p}\cdot|E_{2}|^{1\over q}\cdot|E_{3}|^{1\over{r^{\prime}}},

which is (
405
).

For (
406
), the corresponding size and energy estimates are

\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(f(P_{1}))\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{2}(g(P_{2}))\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{l}}(P_{3}))\lesssim 2^{-\frac{\delta_{1}am}{2}};

and

\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(f(P_{1}))\lesssim|E_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{2}(g(P_{2}))\lesssim|E_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{l}}(P_{3}))\lesssim|E_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We apply again Proposition
7.9
with the same choice of exponents
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3}
, obtaining

\displaystyle\Lambda_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}^{\mathfrak{l}}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle\lesssim|E_{1}|^{{1-\theta_{1}}\over 2}\cdot|E_{2}|^{{1-\theta_{2}}\over 2}\cdot 2^{-\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}am\theta_{3}}|E_{3}|^{{1-\theta_{3}}\over 2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\delta_{1}am(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{r})}|E_{1}|^{1\over p}\cdot|E_{2}|^{1\over q}\cdot|E_{3}|^{1\over{r^{\prime}}};

this is estimate (
406
), where the decaying factor is
2^{-\delta_{1}am(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{r})}
. The proof of (
407
) follows analogously (with the only difference consisting in the estimate
\mathrm{Size\,}^{\mathbb{P}}_{3}(h_{\mathfrak{c}}(P_{3}))\lesssim 2^{-\frac{\mu am}{4}}
), which concludes the proof of the three restricted-type estimates with
\delta_{2}^{\prime}:=\min\{\delta_{1},\frac{\mu}{2}\}(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{r})>0
.

Finally, by invoking restricted-type interpolation (see for example
[
81
]
or
[
44
]
for a reference), we obtain the strong-type estimates of Proposition
7.2
with, say,

\delta_{2}(p,q):=\frac{1}{2}\min\{\delta_{1},\frac{\mu}{2}\}(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{r})>0
.
∎

7.5.2.
Boundedness of
T
in the wider range
\{(p,q,r):2<p,q\leq\infty\ ;\ 1<r<\infty\}

To deal with the extended range and, in particular, to be able to prove some
L^{p}\times L^{\infty}\to L^{p}
endpoint estimates, we need to move outside of the local-
L^{2}
range. As it turns out, it will be sufficient to remove the local-
L^{2}
constraints on the third function
h
. To this aim, we will use the helicoidal method of
[
4
]
, as follows:

(a)

first we transform the time-frequency information obtained in Sections
7.2
-
7.4
into exclusively spatial information (in the form of maximal averages over arbitrary intervals);

(b)

next, we interpolate between this localized information and the localized, exclusively spatial information for an operator resembling a linearized version of the bilinear maximal function from (
111
);

(c)

lastly, we re-organise this information in the form of a Fefferman-Stein inequality from which the extended range of boundedness will follow.

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

To implement the strategy outlined above, we rely on the initial discretization performed in Section
4.2.6
which allows us to represent
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h)
as
\sum_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)
– see (
134
).

For any
\mathbb{P}\subset{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
and
I_{0}
a dyadic interval

\mathbb{P}(I_{0}):=\{P\in\mathbb{P}:I_{P}\subseteq I_{0}\}

denotes the subcollection of
\mathbb{P}
with spatial intervals contained in
I_{0}
. Recalling the definition (
374
), we also define

(408)

\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}:=\{I\subseteq I_{0}\text{ dyadic }:I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}\text{ or }I=I_{0}\}.

In this way, we only keep track of the spatial intervals included in
I_{0}
corresponding to tiles or trees in
\mathbb{P}
, together with the spatial interval
I_{0}
itself.

Item
(a)
above indicates that
|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|
will be estimated using the strategy highlighted in Sections
7.2
-
7.4
, which also produces an exponential decay in
m
; an upper bound for the same expression
|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|
can be obtained directly from (
134
) and its equivalent (
180
), although this time the exponential decay in
m
is not available. Interpolating between the two – as item
(b)
suggests, will produce estimates for
|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|
that fall outside the local-
L^{2}
range and have exponential decay in
m
. It will remain to reconstruct the global information for
|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|
from the local one, and this is achieved via Fefferman-Stein inequalities – this is the last step
(c)
.

Propositions
7.9
,
7.11
, and
7.12
, together with the observation that the energies localize well,
145
145

145

Using the decay of the wave-packets it is not difficult to see that

\mathrm{Energy\,}^{\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}_{1}((f(P_{1}))_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})})\lesssim\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},

and similarly for
g,h
. This resembles somehow the proof of the size estimate (
375
); see also
[
3
, Lemma 39]
.

imply that for some
\tilde{\delta}>0

(409)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\tilde{\delta}am}\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\,\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I_{P}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|h\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\,\cdot|I_{0}|.

Notice that the above estimate represents a localized multi-scale version of the
L^{2}\times L^{2}\times L^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
estimate (
184
) presented in Section
5
. In order to pass from the latter to the former, all the time-frequency discussion performed in Subsections
7.2
and
7.3
, as well as the refinement in Section
6
are necessary.

If we were now to invoke Theorem 12 of
[
4
]
, we could deduce from (
409
) that

(410)

|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h)|\lesssim 2^{-\tilde{\delta}am}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{2}f(x)\cdot M_{2}g(x)\cdot M_{2}h(x)\,dx

(where we recall that
M_{2}f=(M|f|^{2})^{1/2}
