). This inequality is clearly sufficient to deduce the summability of the operator norms of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}
(and hence of
T_{m}
) in the strict local-
L^{2}
range – in this way we would recover the result in Corollary
7.3
. However,
L^{p}\times L^{\infty}\to L^{p}
bounds cannot be concluded from the estimate above.

Identity (
409
) illustrates step
(a)
of the strategy described at the beginning of the present subsection. We continue now with step
(b)
, which consists of obtaining a different type of local estimate for
\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)
.

For this, we rely on the initial definition (
181
) of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)
, but will momentarily return to the parametric formulation based on indices
k,\ell,r,n,p,u,v
. With this,
|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|
becomes

(411)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\frac{am+2k}{4}}}\Big|\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\,\,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)h(x)dx\Big|.

Recalling the definition of
w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)
, the decaying estimate (
140
), and the pointwise inequality

\big|\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)\big|\lesssim 2^{\frac{am-2k}{4}}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x),

we bound (
411
) by

(412)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{k}}\Big|\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\,\,\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x)\frac{1}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{2}}|h(x)|dx.

Notice that the second line does not depend on the
u
parameter anymore since we disregarded the frequency information contained in the wave-packet
\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}
. We can use Cauchy-Schwarz in
u
to obtain, for fixed parameters
v,n,p
,

\displaystyle\sum_{u\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\big|\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\sum_{u_{1}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle f,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},p_{r}-n}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\sum_{u_{2}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle g,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},p_{r}+n}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

So that
|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|
is further bounded by

(413)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{k}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{v\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big(\sum_{u_{1}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle f,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},p_{r}-n}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\sum_{u_{2}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle g,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},p_{r}+n}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\,\,\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x)\frac{1}{1+\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a-1}\lambda(x)}{2^{a(\frac{am}{2}-k)}}+2v\Big|^{2}}|h(x)|dx.

Now the expression in the first line does not depend on
v
anymore, and thus, summing in
v
(see (
386
)), we get

(414)

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|\lesssim\frac{1}{2^{k}}\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{u_{1}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle f,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},p_{r}-n}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\sum_{u_{2}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle g,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},p_{r}+n}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\,\,\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x)|h(x)|dx.

We apply Cauchy-Schwarz with respect to
n
– which only affects the first line – and thus, modulo considering
O(1)
similar terms, we have

(415)

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|\lesssim\frac{1}{2^{k}}

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{n_{1},u_{1}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle f,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{1},n_{1}}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\sum_{u_{2},n_{2}\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\big|\langle g,\check{\varphi}_{\frac{am}{2}-k,\ell}^{u_{2},n_{2}}\rangle\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))\,\,\big(\sum_{p\sim 2^{\frac{am}{2}}}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{k}^{p_{r}}}(x)\big)|h(x)|dx.

At this point, we can return to the notation involving only the tile formulation: for any
P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
, we have proved that

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|I_{P}|}f(P_{1})g(P_{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))\,\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x)|h(x)|dx,

where we recall the notations (
361
) and (
362
).

Putting these together we obtain the local estimate:

\displaystyle\big|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\kappa:2^{\kappa}\leq|I_{0}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq I_{0}\\
|I|=2^{\kappa}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbb{P}\\
I_{P}=I\end{subarray}}\|f_{P}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{P}\|_{L^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-a\kappa}(\lambda(x))\,\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)|h(x)|dx

The tiles in
\mathbb{P}(I_{0})
sharing the same spatial interval correspond to mutually disjoint frequency intervals and due to orthogonality and Cauchy-Schwartz we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})\\
I_{P}=I\end{subarray}}\|f_{P}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{P}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}.

This observation produces

\displaystyle\big|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\,\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa:2^{\kappa}\leq|I_{0}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq I_{0}\\
|J|=2^{\kappa}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|J|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-a\kappa}(\lambda(x))\,\tilde{\chi}_{J}(x)|h(x)|dx.

Since the intervals have the same scale,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq I_{0}\\
|J|=2^{\kappa}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\chi}_{J}(x)\lesssim\tilde{\chi}_{I_{0}}(x);

in addition, thanks to the presence of the stopping-time
\lambda(x)
, for every
x
there are only
O(1)
scales
\kappa
for which
\rho_{am-a\kappa}(\lambda(x))\neq 0
; and therefore

\sum_{\kappa:2^{\kappa}\leq|I_{0}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq I_{0}\\
|J|=2^{\kappa}\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{am-a\kappa}(\lambda(x))\,\tilde{\chi}_{J}(x)|h(x)|dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\chi}_{I_{0}}(x)|h(x)|dx.

We have therefore proven the following bound:

(416)

\displaystyle\big|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\cdot\big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h(x)|\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)dx\big)\,|I_{0}|.

Although the expression involving
h
is a genuine average, for symmetry reasons we express it as a maximal average. Similarly to (
410
),
[
4
, Theorem 12]
implies the global Fefferman-Stein-type inequality

(417)

|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h)|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{2}f(x)\cdot M_{2}g(x)\cdot Mh(x)\,dx.

At this point, we would like to apply a suitable interpolation procedure between (
410
) and (
417
), in a way that avoids canonical interpolation for the operator
T_{m}
.
146
146

146

Estimates of the type
L^{p}\times L^{\infty}\to L^{p}
cannot be obtained through classical interpolation since they correspond to boundary points.

This is achieved by interpolating between the two local estimates (
409
) and (
416
), in the particular situation when
h
is a restricted-type function: i.e.
h
is such that
|h(x)|\leq\mathbf{1}_{H}(x)
for some measurable set
H\subset\mathbb{R}
of finite measure. The maximal averages of
h
in (
409
) are then bounded by

\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}_{H}(x)\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)dx\Big)^{{1\over 2}}

and in (
416
) by

\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}_{H}(x)\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)dx\Big).

Using trivial interpolation (geometric average), we deduce that

(418)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-\tilde{\delta}am\theta}\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I_{P}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)\mathbf{1}_{H}(x)dx\Big)^{{\theta\over 2}+(1-\theta)}\,|I_{0}|,

for any
0<\theta<1
. Then a “mock interpolation” result – see Proposition 14 of
[
4
]
– implies that

(419)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{P\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}(I_{0})}\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-\tilde{\delta}am\theta}\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|g\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{I}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\sup_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}(I_{0})}}\frac{1}{|I|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\chi}_{I}(x)|h(x)|^{\tau}dx\Big)^{1\over\tau}\,|I_{0}|,

for any
\tau>0
such that
\frac{1}{\tau}<1-\frac{\theta}{2}
. We note that the implicit constant blows up as
\tau
nears
\frac{2}{2-\theta}
.

For the last part
(c)
of the strategy, we appeal again to
[
4
, Theorem 12]
, which allows to put back together the information obtained at the local level and deduce – via a sparse domination argument – that

(420)

|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}(f,g,h)|\lesssim 2^{-\tilde{\delta}am\theta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{2}f(x)\cdot M_{2}g(x)\cdot M_{\tau}h(x)\,dx.

This is indeed an interpolation of (
410
) and (
417
), but it is achieved through local control of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}
.

Finally, this implies by duality that with
\bar{\delta}=\tilde{\delta}\theta

\|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m}\|_{L^{p}\times L^{q}\times L^{r^{\prime}}\to{\mathbb{C}}}=\|T_{m}\|_{L^{p}\times L^{q}\to L^{r}}\lesssim 2^{-\bar{\delta}am}

for any
p,q,r
satisfying

\frac{1}{p}<\frac{1}{2},\quad\frac{1}{q}<\frac{1}{2},\quad\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}<1-{\theta\over 2},

additionally to the usual Hölder scaling condition
1/p+1/q=1/r
. Since
\theta\in(0,1)
was arbitrary, these conditions reduce to
2<p,q\leq\infty
and
r^{\prime}>1
.
∎

Remark 7.15
.

(1)

The inequality

\displaystyle|\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|I_{P}|}f(P_{1})g(P_{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))\,\tilde{\chi}_{I_{P}}(x)|h(x)|dx,

is a discretized, dualized version of the estimate

\displaystyle\Big|\rho_{a}(2^{-a(m-k)}\lambda(x))\int f(x-t)g(x+t)e^{i\lambda(x)t^{a}}\rho(2^{-k}t)\frac{dt}{t}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{a}(2^{-a(m-k)}\lambda(x))M(f,g)(x)

used in Section
3
to bound pointwise
T_{m}
by the bilinear maximal function
M
of (
111
). The Gabor frame decomposition used for the higher resolution model naturally induces
L^{2}
norms for the functions
f
and
g
.

(2)

If in (
376
)-(
378
) the size of
h
was an
L^{\tau}
quantity for some
1<\tau<2
, some
L^{p}\times L^{\infty}\to L^{p}
estimates could have been obtained by applying
(a)
directly.
The difference between (
409
) and (
416
) consists in the fact that (
409
) presents a
2^{-\tilde{\delta}am}
decay, but we have to use
L^{2}
maximal averages for
h
, while (
416
) presents no decay in
m
, but the (maximal) averages of
h
are measured relative to the better (smaller)
L^{1}
norm. This dichotomy is explained as follows: The compatibility step in Section
6
, which allows to extract spatial and frequency information for the third function
h
as well, requires a careful distribution of the information on the level set of the oscillatory factor carried by
\rho_{|P|}(\lambda(x))w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)
. Using
\ell^{2}
estimates in the parameters
n,v
and
p^{\prime}
, this information is split between the bilinear term corresponding to
f
and
g
, and the term corresponding to the dualizing function
h
, respectively. Because of this, the sizes of
f,g
and
h
used above in the previous Subsection
7.3
are naturally
L^{2}
-adapted. In obtaining (
416
) however, one may disregard
w_{|P|,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)
altogether exchanging the lack of
m
-decay with the use of
L^{1}
averages for
h
.

Appendix A
Lack of
m
-decaying absolute summability for the discrete phase-linearized wave-packet model

As stated in the Introduction, in this first appendix we prove the lack of the absolute single scale
m
-decay for the discrete phase-linearized wave-packet model defined by (
143
).

We start by recalling the formulations in (
180
) and (
181
) and focus our attention on the expression of this model localized within a fixed tri-tile
P=P(k,\ell,r)\in{{\mathbb{P}}_{\textrm{BHT}}}
given by some prescribed parameters
k,\ell,r\in\mathbb{N}
and a fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,k,\ell,r}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\frac{1}{2^{am+2k\over 4}}\langle f_{\ell+1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u-v,p_{r}-n}\rangle\langle g_{\ell-1},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,\ell}^{u+v,p_{r}+n}\rangle

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2}-k,2\ell-1}^{2u,p_{r}}(x)h(x)\rho_{am-ak}(\lambda(x))w_{k,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)dx.

For convenience we take
P=P_{0}:=P(0,1,1)
so that
k=0
,
\ell=1
,
r=1
,
I_{P^{0}}=[1,2]
,
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{0}_{1}}=2^{am}\,[1,2]
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{P^{0}_{2}}=2^{am}\,[-1,0]
. In what follows we show that if we take absolute values inside the summation signs appearing in the definition of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P}\big|_{P=P_{0}}
above then no analogue of Proposition
5.1
can hold. Moreover, one can not obtain a single scale
m
-decay estimate even within the Hölder scaling
L^{2}\times L^{2}\times L^{\infty}
:

Proposition A.1
.

Fix
147
147

147

The case
a\in\{1,2\}
is superfluous.

a>0
. There are no absolute constants
C,\delta>0
for which the following holds
148
148

148

Here we recall that since
r=1
we must have that
p_{r}=p
.
uniformly in the choices of
m\in\mathbb{N}
(large enough), phase function
\lambda
and input functions
f,g\in L^{2}
,
h\in L^{\infty}
:

(421)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P^{0}}^{abs}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\frac{1}{2^{am\over 4}}|\langle f_{2},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle|\cdot|\langle g_{0},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle|

\displaystyle\cdot\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\rho_{am}(\lambda(x))w_{0,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)dx\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\delta am}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Remark A.2
.

(a) The above proposition remains true if one modifies the norm in which
h
is measured: one can substitute the
L^{\infty}
norm with
L^{2}
or even with Sobolev type norms. The existence of a counterexample at (
421
) is not specific to the control/norm that we put on
h
but rather depends on the interaction between, on the one hand, the information carried by
f
and
g
and, on the other hand, the stricture of the linearizing function
\lambda
;

(b) In fact, our proof reveals the existence of an absolute constant
K>0
so that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
(large enough), there exist nontrivial
f,g\in L^{2}
,
h\in L^{\infty}
and a real measurable phase function
\lambda
for which

\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{m,P^{0}}^{abs}(f,g,h)>K\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Proof.

Our proof will proceed via
reductio ad absurdum
. Thus we assume that for any
f,g\in L^{2}
and
h\in L^{\infty}
there exist
C,\delta>0
absolute constants such that (
421
) holds uniformly in
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough.

Take now

f:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\cdot 2^{am\over 2}\leq|n_{1}|\leq 3\cdot 2^{am\over 2}\\
|p|\leq 3\cdot 2^{am\over 2}\end{subarray}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{n_{1},p}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad g:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|n_{2}|\leq 2^{am\over 2}\\
|p|\leq 3\cdot 2^{am\over 2}\end{subarray}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{n_{2},p}\,.

Thus
f=f_{2}
,
g=g_{0}
and for
n,p,u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}
(suitably understood) we have

\langle f,\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{u-v,p-n}\rangle\sim 1\sim\langle g,\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{u+v,p+n}\rangle

with

\|f\|_{L^{2}}\simeq 2^{am\over 2}\simeq\|g\|_{L^{2}}.

For this specific choice of
f
and
g
we have that (
421
) is equivalent with

(422)

\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big|\int_{I_{P_{0}}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\rho_{am}(\lambda(x))w_{0,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)dx\Big|\leq C2^{-\delta am}2^{\frac{5}{4}am}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Next, we are going to construct the essential item in what will become our counterexample to (
421
) – the phase function
\lambda
. For this we decompose

(423)

\lambda(x)=\lambda_{u}(x)\,+\lambda_{s}(x)\,,

where here

•

the uniform component is defined as
\lambda_{u}(x):=2^{am}
for every
x\in I_{P^{0}}
;

•

the structured component has both the input and the output correlated with some suitable (generalized) arithmetic progressions.
149
149

149

The fact that such a generalized arithmetic progressions structure plays the key rôle in the construction of our present counterexample suggests a natural connection between the (limiting) behavior of
BC^{a}
and that of the Carleson operator
C
. Indeed, same type of structures are essential in providing counterexamples for the behavior of the pointwise convergence of Fourier Series near
L^{1}
– for the latter see
[
74
]
and the bibliography therein.
Indeed, for some
L\in\mathbb{N}
large enough, we take

(424)

\textrm{supp}\,\lambda_{s}=A=\bigcup_{{j\simeq 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}A_{j}\,,

with

(425)

A_{j}:=\bigsqcup_{{q\simeq 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{q\in L\mathbb{N}}}(2^{-am}[j,j+1]+q2^{-\frac{am}{2}})=:\bigsqcup_{{q\simeq 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{q\in L\mathbb{N}}}I_{j,q}\,,

and let

(426)

\lambda_{s}(x):=\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}(2^{am\over 2}\,(j-2^{am\over 2}))\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)\,.

With these, let us record the following facts:

•

by properly adjusting the support of
\rho
we have from the above that
\rho_{am}(\lambda(x)))=1
for every
x\in I_{P^{0}}
;

•

the phase function
\lambda
is constant on each of the subsets
A_{j}
as well as on
B:=I_{P_{0}}\setminus A
;

•

since
w_{0,n,v}^{e}(\lambda_{u})
is constant on the whole interval
I_{P^{0}}
(it does not depend on
x
) it is not hard to see that (
421
) holds for
\lambda=\lambda_{u}
.
150
150

150

This should become transparent once the reader mimics the reasoning presented in (
427
)–(
430
) for the case in which
x\in B
.

From the above we deduce that it is enough to disprove (
422
) for
\rho_{am}(\lambda(x)))=1
and

I_{P_{0}}
replaced by
A
where here
\lambda
and
A
are as constructed above.

We now fix
x\in A
and compute the contribution of the weight defined in (
137
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A}(x)\,w_{0,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\xi+2v)e^{i\,n\,\xi}e^{ic_{a}2^{({am\over 2})a^{\prime}}\xi^{a^{\prime}}\lambda(x)^{-{a^{\prime}\over a}}}\,d\xi

\displaystyle=\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\xi+2v)e^{i\,n\,\xi}e^{ic_{a}2^{({am\over 2})a^{\prime}}\xi^{a^{\prime}}(2^{am\over 2}j)^{-{a^{\prime}\over a}}}\,d\xi\,.

Using now the principle of (non)stationary phase (recall that we are in the regime in which the phase function
\nu
in the integral above satisfies
|\nu^{\prime\prime}(\xi)|\sim 1
on the domain of integration), we have the following approximation:

(427)

\displaystyle w_{0,n,v}^{e}(\lambda)(x)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)e^{i\bar{c}_{a}n^{a}2^{am\over 2}j2^{-a\left(\frac{am}{2}\right)}}\psi\big(\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}2^{am\over 2}j}{2^{a\left(\frac{am}{2}\right)}}+2v\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)e^{i\bar{c}_{a}n^{a}j2^{-\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}\psi\big(\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}j}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}+2v\big).

Indeed, the above holds up to an error term which involves a decaying factor of the form
2^{-\delta am}
for some
\delta>0
thus not affecting the construction of our counterexample below.

Consequently, in order to disprove (
422
) it is enough to show that there are no
C,\,\delta>0
such that the following holds uniformly in
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
h\in L^{\infty}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{u,v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big|\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)dx\big)e^{i\bar{c}_{a}n^{a}j2^{-\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}\psi\big(\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}j}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}+2v\big)\Big|

(428)

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\delta am}\,2^{\frac{5}{4}am}\,\|h\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Recalling that
\psi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and choosing appropriately its support, we deduce that in order for the inner summation term in (
428
) to be nonzero we must have

(429)

\Big|\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}j}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}+2v\Big|\leq 1

which, since
n\sim 2^{am\over 2}
, is equivalent to

\Big|j+\frac{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}+1}}{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}}v\Big|\leq\frac{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}{\bar{c}_{a}n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}}\sim 1\>.

Hence for
n
fixed,
j
is determined by
v
since
j\in L\mathbb{N}
and
L\in\mathbb{N}
may be chosen as large as we want (but still independently of
m
). Thus, without loss of generality we may assume that (
429
) describes a
1
-to-
1
map
v\leftrightarrow j
.

Fixing
n\sim 2^{am\over 2}
, we have from the above that

(430)

\displaystyle\sum_{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\Big|\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)dx\Big)e^{i\bar{c}_{a}n^{a}j2^{-\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}\psi\big(\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}j}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}+2v\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\Big|\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)dx\Big)e^{i\bar{c}_{a}n^{a}j2^{-\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}\psi\big(\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}j}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}+2v\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{v\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\Big|\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}(x)h(x)\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}(x)dx\Big)\Big|\Big|\psi\big(\bar{c}_{a}\frac{n^{a\over{a^{\prime}}}j}{2^{\frac{a^{2}m}{2a^{\prime}}}}+2v\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\gtrsim\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\big|\langle h\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}\rangle\big|.

Notice that the last lower bound does not depend on the parameter
n
.

So in order for (
428
) to be true, we must have

(431)

\sum_{u,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\sum_{{j\sim 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{j\in L\mathbb{N}}}\big|\langle h\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}\rangle\big|\leq C2^{-\delta am}2^{\frac{3}{4}am}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Remark A.3
.

Observe that the trivial case
\delta=0
in (
431
) is an immediate consequence of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality via the pairwise disjointness of
\{A_{j}\}_{j}
and the almost orthogonality of
\big\{\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}\big\}_{u,p}
. Thus, in order to maximize the left hand-side of (
431
) it is natural to pursue the equality case in the above Cauchy-Schwarz argument thus constructing
\{A_{j}\}_{j}
and
h\in L^{\infty}
such that the local Fourier coefficients
\big|\langle h\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}\rangle\big|
are (essentially) pairwise equal and hence independent of the specific choice of
u,p,j
. In particular, this explains why we design the structure of
(A_{j})_{j}
as essentially a
2^{-am}
-neighborhood of a generalized arithmetic progression of rank 2 (hence its “
2^{-\frac{am}{2}}
–translation invariance behavior” in (
425
)).

Take now
151
151

151

Or some smooth variant of it which is still equal to
1
on
[1,2]
.

h=\mathbf{1}_{[1,2]}
and recall the definition of
A_{j}
in (
425
). By properly massaging the properties of
\check{\varphi}
we can assume without loss of generality that for every
j\sim 2^{am\over 2}
with
j\in L\mathbb{N}
we have that

(432)

\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}x-q)\sim 1\qquad\textrm{for any}\qquad x\in I_{j,q}\,,

and

(433)

|\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}x-q)|\leq|q-q^{\prime}|^{-10}\qquad\textrm{for any}\>\>x\in I_{j,q^{\prime}}\>\>\textrm{with}\>\>\>q,q^{\prime}\in L\mathbb{Z},\>q\not=q^{\prime}\,.

In what follows we fix
u,p\sim 2^{am\over 2}
. From the above we then deduce that

\displaystyle\big|\langle h\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}},\check{\varphi}_{{am\over 2},1}^{2u,p}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=2^{\frac{am}{4}}\Big|\int_{A_{j}}\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}x-p)\,e^{i\,2^{\frac{am}{2}}x\left(2u+2^{\frac{am}{2}}\right)}dx\Big|

\displaystyle=2^{\frac{am}{4}}\Big|\sum_{{{q\simeq 2^{am\over 2}}\atop{q\in L\mathbb{N}}}}\int_{I_{j,q}}\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}x-p)\,e^{i\,2^{\frac{am}{2}}x\left(2u+2^{\frac{am}{2}}\right)}dx\Big|

\displaystyle\geq 2^{\frac{am}{4}}\Big|\int_{I_{j,p}}\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}x-p)e^{i\,2^{\frac{am}{2}}x\left(2u+2^{\frac{am}{2}}\right)}dx\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2^{\frac{am}{4}}\,\sum_{{q\neq p}\atop{q\in L\mathbb{Z}}}\Big|\int_{I_{j,q}}\check{\varphi}(2^{\frac{am}{2}}x-p)e^{i\,2^{\frac{am}{2}}x\left(2u+2^{\frac{am}{2}}\right)}dx\Big|

(434)

\displaystyle=:(I)-(II).

For the first term, applying (
432
) and a change of variable, we claim that
152
152

152

Recall our earlier convention
e^{iy}=e^{2\pi iy}
.

(435)

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}\Big|\int_{[0,1]}\check{\varphi}(2^{-\frac{am}{2}}(j+y))e^{iy\left(2^{-\frac{am}{2}+1}u+1\right)}dy\Big|

\displaystyle\simeq 2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}\Big|\check{\varphi}(2^{-\frac{am}{2}}j)\int_{[0,1]}e^{iy\left(2^{-\frac{am}{2}+1}u+1\right)}dy\Big|\simeq 2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}

holds for a large fraction of the possible values of
u\sim 2^{am\over 2}
.

Indeed, for this we first notice that while
u
runs through the set
{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[2^{am\over 2},2\cdot 2^{am\over 2}]
, the expression
2^{-\frac{am}{2}+1}u+1
covers the set
2^{-{am\over 2}+1}{\mathbb{Z}}+1\cap[3,5]
. Thus, for
m\in\mathbb{N}
large enough, (say
am\geq 10
), there exists a value of
u=u_{0}
in the range
u_{0}\sim 2^{am\over 2}
such that

\text{dist}(2^{-\frac{am}{2}+1}u_{0}+1,{\mathbb{Z}})\geq\frac{1}{10}.

Then an easy argument shows that for
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[2^{am\over 2},2\cdot 2^{am\over 2}]\cap[u_{0}-\frac{1}{100}2^{am\over 2},u_{0}+\frac{1}{100}2^{am\over 2}]
one still has

\text{dist}(2^{-\frac{am}{2}+1}u+1,{\mathbb{Z}})\geq\frac{1}{4}

which implies the lower bound in (
435
)

|\int_{[0,1]}e^{iy\left(2^{-\frac{am}{2}+1}u+1\right)}dy|\geq\frac{1}{100}.

Hence, for
u
belonging to
{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[2^{am\over 2},2\cdot 2^{am\over 2}]\cap[u_{0}-\frac{1}{100}2^{am\over 2},u_{0}+\frac{1}{100}2^{am\over 2}]
one can now verify the validity of the statement

(436)

(I)\geq 10^{-10}\,2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}.

For the second part
(II)
we are using (
433
) for, say, a choice of
L\geq 100
. With this we have

(437)

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}\sum_{{q\neq p}\atop{q\in L\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{[0,1]}|\check{\varphi}(2^{-\frac{am}{2}}(j+y)+(p-q))|dy

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}\sum_{d\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}(1+|d|\,L)^{-10}\leq 2^{-\frac{3am}{4}}\,100\,L^{-10}.

Putting now together (
A
)–(
437
) we obtain a refutation of (
431
) thus contradicting the assumption that (
421
) holds.
∎

Appendix B
Analysis of the multiplier

In this appendix we perform the last part of the initial reductions, the result of which was anticipated in Subsection
3.5
: namely that the initial symbol (
116
) is equal to a main term of the form (
117
) plus an error term which is suitably bounded. This is a rather standard non-stationary phase argument
153
153

153

A classical reference for this type of stationary phase analysis is provided by
[
95
]
.
, applied to our specific setting.

We start by recalling our symbol

\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)=\int e^{i\lambda t^{a}}\frac{\rho(2^{-k}t)}{t}e^{-i\zeta t}\,dt.

The analysis of the above expression proceeds as follows: first one reduces the matters to the subset of parameters
(\zeta,\lambda)
for which the phase contains a stationary point (this produces controlled errors of non-stationary nature); secondly, one specializes the amplitude of the symbol to a sufficiently small neighbourhood of the stationary point (this produces further controlled errors of non-stationary nature); thirdly, one applies the stationary phase method to the remaining part, obtaining the main contribution to the oscillatory integral
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}
; finally, the resulting symbol is put into a tensorized form by a standard Fourier series decomposition argument. The errors that have arisen along the way are all controlled by the bilinear maximal function – more precisely, the error contribution to
T_{m}(f,g)(x)
will be controlled pointwise by
\lesssim 2^{-\delta am}M(f,g)(x)
for some absolute
\delta>0
– any such
\delta
is good enough for our purposes.

B.1.
Non-stationary contributions to
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}
when there is no critical point

First of all we tackle the non-stationary parts of the symbol. In what follows we will always assume that
\lambda\equiv\lambda(x)\sim 2^{a(m-k)}
because of the presence of the factor
\tilde{\rho}(2^{-a(m-k)}\lambda(x))
in the expression for
T_{m}(f,g)
.

If
\varphi(t)=\varphi_{\zeta,\lambda}(t):=\lambda t^{a}-\zeta t
denotes the phase of
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)
, a simple dimensional analysis shows that in the domain of integration we have
|\lambda t^{a-1}|\sim 2^{am-k}
, and therefore
\varphi^{\prime}
will certainly be non-zero if
|\zeta|\not\sim 2^{am-k}
; the non-stationary phase principle then suggests that the symbol will be suitably small for such
\zeta
. To make this intuition rigorous, we begin by observing that by integration by parts

(438)

\displaystyle\int e^{i\lambda t^{a}}

\displaystyle\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}e^{-i\zeta t}\,dt=-\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\frac{d}{dt}\Big(\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{it\varphi^{\prime}(t)}\Big)\,dt

\displaystyle=-\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\Big[\frac{2^{-k}{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)}{it\varphi^{\prime}(t)}-\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{it^{2}\varphi^{\prime}(t)}-\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t)}{it(\varphi^{\prime}(t))^{2}}\Big]\,dt.

Recall that
\rho
as given in (
107
) is smooth, compactly supported on
\Big[\frac{1}{2},2\Big]
and such that
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\rho(2^{-j}\zeta)=1
for
\zeta\neq 0
. Let us then decompose smoothly
1=\chi_{0}+\chi_{1}+\sum_{j\geq 2}\chi_{j}
, where

i)

\chi_{1}(\zeta):=\sum_{\ell=-2\lceil a\rceil}^{2\lceil a\rceil}\rho(a^{-1}2^{-\ell}\zeta)
;

ii)

\chi_{j}(\zeta):=\rho(a^{-1}2^{-2\lceil a\rceil+1}2^{-j}\zeta)
for any
j\geq 2
;

iii)

\chi_{0}=1-\chi_{1}-\sum_{j\geq 2}\chi_{j}
.

Observe that
\chi_{0}
is a bump function supported in a neighbourhood of the origin, and more precisely it is supported in
\{\zeta:|\zeta|<a2^{-2\lceil a\rceil}\}
.

We can then split

\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)=\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)+\Big(1-\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\Big)\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)

and further split the last term (which will be shown to contribute an error term) according to (
438
) into

\Big(1-\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\Big)\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)=-\mathfrak{E}^{1}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)+\mathfrak{E}^{2}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)+\mathfrak{E}^{3}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda),

where for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
the term
\mathfrak{E}^{i}_{m,k}
is given by the sum

\mathfrak{E}^{i}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda):=\mathfrak{E}^{i,0}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)+\sum_{j\geq 2}\mathfrak{E}^{i,j}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)

and for
j=0
or
j\geq 2
we have set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{1,j}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\chi_{j}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\frac{{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)}{it}\frac{2^{-k}}{\varphi^{\prime}(t)}\,dt,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{2,j}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\chi_{j}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{it}\frac{1}{t\varphi^{\prime}(t)}\,dt,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{3,j}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\chi_{j}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{it}\frac{\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t)}{(\varphi^{\prime}(t))^{2}}\,dt.

Let us begin with showing that

(439)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)

\displaystyle e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\mathfrak{E}^{1,0}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|,

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-am}M(f,g)(x),

so that this contribution to
T_{m}(f,g)
can be safely ignored (thanks to the fact that the bilinear maximal function is bounded in the range given in Theorem
3.1
). Considering
\mathfrak{E}^{1,0}_{m,k}
, we see that for the factor
2^{-k}/\varphi^{\prime}(t)
we can write

\frac{2^{-k}}{\varphi^{\prime}(t)}=2^{-am}\frac{1}{a\lambda t^{a-1}2^{-(am-k)}-\zeta 2^{-(am-k)}},

and if we let

\Phi_{\lambda,t}(\zeta^{\prime})=\Phi_{m,k,\lambda,t}(\zeta^{\prime}):=\frac{\chi_{0}(\zeta^{\prime})}{a\lambda t^{a-1}2^{-(am-k)}-\zeta^{\prime}}

we can rewrite

\mathfrak{E}^{1,0}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)=2^{-am}\int\Phi_{\lambda,t}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)e^{-i\zeta t}\frac{{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)}{it}e^{i\lambda t^{a}}\,dt.

Notice that
|a\lambda t^{a-1}2^{-(am-k)}|\sim_{a}1
and more precisely that

\displaystyle a2^{-2a+1}

\displaystyle\leq|a\lambda t^{a-1}2^{-(am-k)}|\leq a2^{2a-1}\qquad

when
a>1
,

\displaystyle\frac{a}{2}

\displaystyle\leq|a\lambda t^{a-1}2^{-(am-k)}|\leq 2a

when
a<1
.

For
\zeta^{\prime}
in the support of
\chi_{0}
, we then have in both cases
a>1
and
a<1
that

|a\lambda t^{a-1}2^{-(am-k)}-\zeta^{\prime}|\gtrsim_{a}1.

From this, one can see that
\Phi_{\lambda,t}
is a smooth bump function adapted to an interval of length
\sim 1
uniformly in all the parameters
m,k,\lambda\sim 2^{a(m-k)},|t|\sim 2^{k}
, and in particular we have uniformly

(440)

|\widehat{\Phi_{\lambda,t}}(s)|\lesssim_{a}(1+|s|)^{-100}

for all
|t|\sim 2^{k}
. All implicit constants here are allowed to depend on
a
, so from this point on we do not track the dependence systematically. Fixing
m,k,\lambda(x)\sim 2^{a(m-k)}
and using Fubini twice, we see by going back to the spatial representation of the operator that we have

\displaystyle\Big|

\displaystyle\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\mathfrak{E}^{1,0}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|

\displaystyle=2^{-am}\Big|\int f(x-s)g(x+s)\Big[\int 2^{am-k}\widehat{\Phi_{\lambda,t}}(2^{am-k}(s-t))\frac{{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)}{it}e^{i\lambda t^{a}}\,dt\Big]\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-am}\int|f(x-s)||g(x+s)|\int 2^{am-k}\big|\widehat{\Phi_{\lambda,t}}(2^{am-k}(s-t))\big|\frac{|{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)|}{|t|}\,dt\,ds.

Using (
440
) we obtain for the resulting kernel

\displaystyle\int

\displaystyle 2^{am-k}\big|\widehat{\Phi_{\lambda,t}}(2^{am-k}(s-t))\big|\frac{|{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)|}{|t|}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int 2^{am-k}\Big(1+2^{am-k}|s-t|\Big)^{-100}\frac{|{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)|}{|t|}\,dt\lesssim 2^{-k}(1+2^{-k}|s|)^{-50},

where we have used the fact that
{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)/t=2^{-k}{\rho}^{\prime}(2^{-k}t)/(2^{-k}t)
is an
L^{1}
-normalised bump function adapted to
|t|\sim 2^{k}
. Thus we have shown

\displaystyle\Big|

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\mathfrak{E}^{1,0}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-am}\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\int|f(x-s)||g(x+s)|2^{-k}(1+2^{-k}|s|)^{-50}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-am}\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)M(f,g)(x);

summing over
k\in\mathbb{Z}
shows the desired pointwise inequality (
439
).

For
\mathfrak{E}^{1,j}_{m,k}
and
j\geq 2
the situation is entirely similar but we have summability in
j
as well: one can write

\chi_{j}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\frac{2^{-k}}{\varphi^{\prime}(t)}=2^{-am}2^{-j}\Phi_{\lambda,j,t}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)

for
\Phi_{\lambda,j,t}
a smooth bump function adapted to an interval of length
\sim 2^{j}
uniformly in
j
and
|t|\sim 2^{k}
(and the rest of the parameters); in particular we have

|\widehat{\Phi_{\lambda,j,t}}(s)|\lesssim 2^{j}(1+2^{j}|s|)^{-100}

uniformly in
j
and in
|t|\sim 2^{k}
, which replaces (
440
). The same argument given above then shows that for any
j\geq 2

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{1,j}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-am}2^{-j}M(f,g)(x),

which is summable in
j
and therefore we have shown that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{1}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-am}M(f,g)(x),

and this whole contribution to
T_{m}(f,g)
can be safely ignored.

The error terms with symbols
\mathfrak{E}^{2}_{m,k},\mathfrak{E}^{3}_{m,k}
can be treated analogously: one just repeats the arguments given above with
2^{-k}/\varphi^{\prime}(t)
replaced by
1/(t\varphi^{\prime}(t))
and
\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t)/(\varphi^{\prime}(t))^{2}
– which can be seen to behave in exactly the same way for the purposes of the above argument (we stress that the presence of the factor of
{\rho}(2^{-k}t)
forces
|t|\sim 2^{k}
). Thus we can dispense with these error terms as well and have effectively reduced ourselves to the study of the bilinear operator with multiplier symbol
\chi_{1}(\zeta 2^{-(am-k)})\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)
.

B.2.
Non-stationary contributions to
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}
close to the critical point

At this point the phase
\varphi(t)=\lambda t^{a}-\zeta t
can have one or two critical points
154
154

154

In the latter case they agree in absolute value.
: we denote either one of these critical points by
t_{0}
and observe that

|t_{0}|=\Big(\frac{|\zeta|}{a|\lambda|}\Big)^{1/(a-1)}\sim 2^{-k},

since in what follows we require
|\zeta|\sim 2^{am-k}
. We will further resolve the symbol by decomposing the domain of integration in
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}
according to a Whitney decomposition relative to
t_{0}
: when we are not too close,
|\varphi^{\prime}|
is still large enough to produce decay while when we get too close, we split the phase into its quadratic part and the remainder Taylor tail with the former giving rise to the main term while the latter representing an error term.

We proceed now by splitting the function
{\rho}
into two functions, one supported in the positive reals and one supported in the negative reals (with a slight abuse of notation we will continue to denote either one of them
{\rho}
). Next, we assume without loss of generality that there is a critical point
\varphi^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
and that
t,t_{0},\zeta>0
. In this case we write

t_{0}=t_{0}(\zeta;\lambda)=\Big(\frac{\zeta}{a\lambda}\Big)^{1/(a-1)}

and notice that
t_{0}
is smooth as a function of
\zeta
. We have
|t_{0}|\sim 2^{k}
as remarked before and therefore
|t-t_{0}|\lesssim 2^{k}
for all
t
in the support of
{\rho}(2^{-k}t)
, so if we use
{\rho}_{0}
to denote the same function as in (
107
) (a smooth bump function supported in an annulus) we can decompose in
j
as

\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt=\sum_{j:2^{j}\lesssim 2^{k}}\int e^{i\varphi(t)}{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt.

We first restrict ourselves to those
j
for which
2^{-am/3+k}\lesssim 2^{j}\lesssim 2^{k}
(the reason for this particular choice will be clear a posteriori) and claim that all these terms actually give rise to error terms - by the same arguments used in
B.1
. Indeed, using integration by parts on

(441)

\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi(t)}{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt

we obtain a number of terms of similar nature; we consider here for shortness only the term

\mathcal{E}^{j}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda):=\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi(t)}\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{it}{{\rho}_{0}}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{2^{-j}}{\varphi^{\prime}(t)}\,dt

which provides the largest contribution to the error term - the other terms are all treated similarly. Applying the argument used in
B.1
together with the fact that

(442)

\displaystyle|\varphi^{\prime}(t)|=a\lambda\Big|t^{a-1}-t_{0}(\zeta)^{a-1}\Big|

\displaystyle=a\lambda\Big|\int_{t_{0}(\zeta)}^{t}(a-1)s^{a-2}\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle\sim_{a}2^{a(m-k)}|t-t_{0}(\zeta)|2^{(a-2)k}\sim_{a}2^{am-2k+j},

we obtain that

\Big|\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\mathcal{E}^{j}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim 2^{-am}2^{2k-2j}M(f,g)(x).

Summing over the
2^{-am/3+k}\lesssim 2^{j}\lesssim 2^{k}
range we obtain then an error contribution bounded by
\lesssim 2^{-am/3}M(f,g)(x)
, which is acceptable. The other contributions arisen from the integration by parts can be shown to be controlled by
\lesssim 2^{-2am/3}M(f,g)(x)
, and thus we have completed this intermediate reduction.

Finally, we deal with the terms of the form (
441
) for those
j
such that
2^{j}\lesssim 2^{-am/3+k}
. These terms contain the main contribution to
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}
, but to properly isolate this we need to remove some further error terms. Expanding the phase in Taylor series around the critical point, we let

\varphi_{0}(t):=\varphi(t_{0})+\frac{\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})}{2}(t-t_{0})^{2}

denote the quadratic part of the phase (
\varphi^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
by definition) and
\widetilde{\varphi}(t):=\varphi(t)-\varphi_{0}(t)
denote the tail. We decompose for any
j

\displaystyle\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\int e^{i\varphi(t)}{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt

(443)

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi_{0}(t)}{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt

(444)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\underbrace{\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi_{0}(t)}(e^{i\widetilde{\varphi}(t)}-1)\tilde{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{\tilde{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt}_{\textstyle=:\mathscr{E}^{j}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)}

and claim that (
444
) represents an error term. Indeed, for
t
in the support of
{\rho}_{0}(2^{-j}(t-t_{0}))
, we observe that
155
155

155

Here we are using the real analyticity of
\widetilde{\varphi}
on any given compact interval that does not contain the origin.

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\varphi}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\geq 3}\frac{|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\ell)}(t_{0})|}{\ell!}|t-t_{0}|^{\ell}\lesssim\sum_{\ell\geq 3}\frac{2^{a(m-k)}2^{(a-\ell)k}}{\ell!}2^{j\ell}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{am}\sum_{\ell\geq 3}2^{-(k-j)\ell}\lesssim 2^{am-3k+3j},

and this is
\ll 1
when
2^{j}\lesssim 2^{-am/3+k}
(this is the reason for our choice of cutoff in the previous argument). A similar estimate shows that we have

|{\widetilde{\varphi}}^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim 2^{am-4k+3j}

as well; if we let
\nu(t):=e^{i\widetilde{\varphi}(t)}-1
, the trivial inequality
|e^{is}-1|\leq|s|
shows that we have

(445)

|\nu(t)|\lesssim 2^{am-3k+3j}\hskip 40.00006pt\text{ and }\hskip 40.00006pt|\nu^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim 2^{am-3k+3j}\cdot 2^{-k}

and analogous bounds for higher derivatives. We also observe that a simple computation shows

(446)

|{\varphi_{0}}^{\prime}(t)|\sim 2^{am-2k+j}

(the same bound one has for
\varphi^{\prime}
; see (
442
)). After integrating (
444
) by parts, one can feed these estimates to the – by now usual – argument of
B.1
to show that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\Big(\sum_{j:2^{j}\lesssim 2^{-am/3+k}}\mathscr{E}^{j}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\Big)\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-am/3}M(f,g)(x),

which is another acceptable error term.

We see that we have effectively reduced our task to that of treating the bilinear operator with multiplier symbol given by terms (
443
), or more precisely

\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi_{0}(t)}\Big[\sum_{j:2^{j}\lesssim 2^{-am/3+k}}{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\Big]\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt.

It is a little inconvenient to have the cut-off factor in square brackets in the expression, and thus we remove it by writing the above as

(447)

\displaystyle\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi_{0}(t)}\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt

(448)

\displaystyle-\sum_{j:2^{-am/3+k}\lesssim 2^{j}\lesssim 2^{k}}\chi_{1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)\int e^{i\varphi_{0}(t)}{\rho}_{0}\Big(\frac{t-t_{0}(\zeta)}{2^{j}}\Big)\frac{{\rho}(2^{-k}t)}{t}\,dt;

a repetition of the arguments given above
156
156

156

Notice that phases
\varphi
and
\varphi_{0}
satisfy the same estimates.
shows that the contribution to
T_{m}(f,g)(x)
from (
448
) amounts to another error term dominated by
\lesssim 2^{-am/3}M(f,g)(x)
. What is left is (
447
), which is the main contribution that will be analysed below.

B.3.
Stationary contributions to
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}

We have managed to reduce the multiplier symbol from
\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}
to (
447
), where the difference between the two amounts to the fact that the phase is now purely quadratic and we have localised the multiplier symbol to
|\zeta|\sim 2^{am-k}
. The advantage is that we can now appeal to the identity
157
157

157

The formula is well-known; it is an easy consequence of the formula for the Fourier transform of a Gaussian and of an analytic continuation argument. Recall also our convention in footnote
4
.

\int e^{i\mu t^{2}}\phi(t)\,dt=\Big(\frac{i}{2\mu}\Big)^{1/2}\int e^{-i\alpha^{2}/(4\mu)}\,\widehat{\phi}(\alpha)\,d\alpha

for (say) Schwartz functions
\phi
in order to produce explicit formulas for the multiplier symbol. Indeed, if we let
\theta(s):={\rho}(s)/s
and apply the above identity to the integral expression in (
447
) we obtain (with a change of variables) the expression

(449)

e^{i\varphi(t_{0})}\Big(\frac{i}{2^{2k}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})}\Big)^{1/2}\int e^{-i\alpha^{2}/(2^{2k+1}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0}))}e^{i(t_{0}/2^{k})\alpha}\,\widehat{\theta}(\alpha)\,d\alpha.

Easy computations–with
c_{a}:=a^{-a/(a-1)}-a^{-1/(a-1)}
–yield that

\displaystyle\varphi(t_{0})

\displaystyle=c_{a}\zeta^{\frac{a}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}},

\displaystyle\frac{t_{0}}{2^{k}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{a-1}}\Big(\frac{a\lambda}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{a-1}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{2k}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-am}}{a-1}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)^{-\frac{a-2}{a-1}}\Big(\frac{a\lambda}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}.

The latter in particular shows that, in the integration region
|\alpha|\lesssim 1
where most of the mass of
\widehat{\theta}(\alpha)
is concentrated, the quadratic phase
\alpha^{2}/(2^{2k+1}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0}))
is extremely small (in particular it is
\lesssim 2^{-am}
). To take advantage of this we split (
449
) as

(450)

\displaystyle e^{i\varphi(t_{0})}\Big(\frac{i}{2^{2k}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})}\Big)^{1/2}\int e^{i(t_{0}/2^{k})\alpha}\,\widehat{\theta}(\alpha)\,d\alpha

(451)

\displaystyle+e^{i\varphi(t_{0})}\Big(\frac{i}{2^{2k}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})}\Big)^{1/2}\int\big(e^{-i\alpha^{2}/(2^{2k+1}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0}))}-1\big)e^{i(t_{0}/2^{k})\alpha}\,\widehat{\theta}(\alpha)\,d\alpha.

Expression (
450
) is clearly equal to

e^{i\varphi(t_{0})}\Big(\frac{i}{2^{2k}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})}\Big)^{1/2}\theta\Big(\frac{t_{0}}{2^{k}}\Big),

which will give our main term, while (
451
) (we claim) contributes another error term. If we define the smooth function

\Theta(\zeta,\lambda):=\frac{\tilde{\rho}(a^{-1}\lambda)\chi_{1}(\zeta)}{\big(\zeta^{\frac{a-2}{a-1}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{a-1}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\theta\Big(\Big(\frac{\zeta}{\lambda}\Big)^{\frac{1}{a-1}}\Big)

(compactly supported in
|\zeta|\sim|\lambda|\sim 1
), then the main term in
\tilde{\rho}(\lambda 2^{-a(m-k)})\mathfrak{M}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)
is (a constant multiple of)

(452)

\mathfrak{m}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda):=2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\;e^{ic_{a}\zeta^{\frac{a}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}}\;\Theta\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}},\frac{a\lambda}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big).

It remains to prove our claim about the error term nature of (
451
). Observe that with a little more algebra this remaining contribution to the symbol can be written as

\mathfrak{e}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda):=2^{-\frac{am}{2}}\;e^{ic_{a}\zeta^{\frac{a}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}}\;\tilde{\Theta}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}},\frac{a\lambda}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big)\Psi_{m}\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}},\frac{a\lambda}{2^{a(m-k)}}\Big),

where we have defined

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\tilde{\rho}(a^{-1}\lambda)\chi_{1}(\zeta)}{\big(\zeta^{\frac{a-2}{a-1}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{a-1}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{m}(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\int\big(e^{ic^{\prime}_{a}2^{-am}\zeta^{-\frac{a-2}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}\alpha^{2}}-1\big)e^{i\zeta^{\frac{1}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}\alpha}\;\widehat{\theta}(\alpha)\,d\alpha

(and
c^{\prime}_{a}
is another constant that depends on
a
). We have that

\displaystyle\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\mathfrak{e}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda)\,d\xi\,d\eta

\displaystyle=\int f(x-s)g(x+s)\Big[\int\mathfrak{e}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)e^{-i\zeta s}\,d\zeta\Big]\,ds;

the oscillatory integral expression in square brackets has phase
c_{a}\zeta^{\frac{a}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}-\zeta s
and, thanks to the support properties of
\tilde{\Theta}
, there will be a critical point only if
|s|\sim 2^{k}
. Splitting the domain of integration into
|s|\sim 2^{k}
and its complement, it is easy to show (using the trivial inequality
|e^{i\alpha}-1|\leq|\alpha|
and the rapid decay of
|\widehat{\theta}(\alpha)|
) that, say,

|\tilde{\Theta}(\zeta,\lambda)\Psi_{m}(\zeta,\lambda)|\lesssim 2^{-(3/4)am},

and therefore one can bound in the former case

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|s|\sim 2^{k}}f(x-s)g(x+s)\Big[\int\mathfrak{e}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda)e^{-i\zeta s}\,d\zeta\Big]\,ds\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|s|\sim 2^{k}}|f(x-s)||g(x+s)|2^{-\frac{am}{2}}2^{am-k}2^{-(3/4)am}\,ds

\displaystyle=2^{-am/4}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\int_{|s|\sim 2^{k}}|f(x-s)||g(x+s)|\,ds\lesssim 2^{-\frac{am}{4}}M(f,g)(x),

an acceptable contribution. In the complementary case (
|s|\not\sim 2^{k}
) we find ourselves with a non-stationary phase situation, which can be treated by a dyadic decomposition in
|s|
and by means of integration by parts. The resulting elementary argument is quite analogous to that in
B.1
, albeit longer and possibly even more tedious - hence we omit the unnecessary details. Overall we have shown that

\Big|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}{\mathfrak{e}}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta\Big|\lesssim 2^{-\delta am}M(f,g)(x),

for some absolute
\delta>0
thus proving our claim that
\mathfrak{m}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda)
is the main contribution to the multiplier symbol.

B.4.
Tensorization of the main contribution

The function
\Theta
in (
452
) is very well-behaved: it is smooth and compactly supported in
\{(\zeta,\lambda)\,:\,|\zeta|\sim 1\>\>\textrm{and}\>\>|\lambda|\sim 1\}
. However it is not in the most convenient form, since currently it is defined as a function that depends on
\zeta
and
\lambda
simultaneously. It will be more convenient to separate the two variables by means of a tensorization argument. Indeed, by a standard double Fourier series decomposition one can rewrite the function
\Theta
as

\Theta(\zeta,\lambda)=\sum_{j,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{j,\ell}\rho_{j}(\lambda)\psi_{\ell}(\zeta),

where
\rho_{j}(\lambda),\psi_{\ell}(\zeta)
are smooth bump functions supported in
|\lambda|\sim 1
and
|\zeta|\sim 1
respectively which satisfy

\|\rho_{j}^{(n)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{n}|j|^{n},\quad\|\psi_{\ell}^{(n)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{n}|\ell|^{n}

for sufficiently many derivatives and the coefficients
c_{j,\ell}
are fastly decaying. By triangle/Hölder inequality it is thus sufficient
158
158

158

Provided that the
L^{p}\times L^{q}\to L^{r}
bounds thus obtained depend at most polynomially on the
C^{1}
norms of
\rho,\psi
.
to prove Theorem
3.2
for the operators

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rho}\Big(\frac{\lambda(x)}{2^{-a(m-k)}}\Big)\iint\widehat{f}(\xi)\widehat{g}(\eta)e^{i(\xi+\eta)x}\mathfrak{m}_{m,k}(\xi-\eta;\lambda(x))\,d\xi\,d\eta,

where we have redefined
\mathfrak{m}_{m,k}
to be

\mathfrak{m}_{m,k}(\zeta;\lambda):=2^{-am/2}\;e^{ic_{a}\zeta^{\frac{a}{a-1}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{a-1}}}\;\psi\Big(\frac{\zeta}{2^{am-k}}\Big)

and
\rho,\psi
are now
generic
smooth bump functions supported in
|\lambda|\sim 1,|\zeta|\sim 1
respectively. This is precisely the form of the multiplier symbol given in (
117
), as desired.

Remark B.1
.

This last tensorization step is where the smoothness of the function
\tilde{\rho}
has finally come in handy. All the previous steps in the reduction could have equally been achieved with a characteristic function in place of
\tilde{\rho}
, but the resulting function
\Theta
would not have been smooth in
\lambda
.
